**DA-41 Lint run points, the one config file and determinism.** The full content lint runs offline and in CI (the project's gdUnit4 command line; the `tests/` tree and `addons/gdUnit4` do not exist yet, IDB OQ 28, so this is stated as the target). At BOOTING the game re-checks only six rules: `PLACEMENT_DEF_UNKNOWN`, `SELF_POWERED_LAMP_COUNT` (EM:665: the authoring lint and boot refuse any other self-powered fixture; the dock status glows of DA-46 are counted by `DOCK_GLOW_COUNT` instead, recognised by their `node_ref` list until the Emitter's amendment names their class, so BOOT never refuses them, OQ-DA-46, user ruling X2 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*), `BEACON_COUNT`, `STORY_SLOT_LIST` (Layout's), `RESIDUAL_LIGHT_COUNT` and `DEAD_FAULT_NOT_DRIED`. The structural lint is Layout's BOOTING pass and is cited, never re-listed. A failed CI content lint never bricks a release boot beyond that subset. The lint is a **pure function of the files plus one named config**: every threshold it reads lives in `content_lint_config`, rows `{name, value, owner, status in {LOCKED, PLACEHOLDER}}`; `TUNING_SOURCE_DRIFT` compares each LOCKED row with the registry and each PLACEHOLDER row with the owner GDD's current text, so a placeholder cannot silently become a fact. The PLACEHOLDER rows this GDD reads from other owners (each a `content_lint_config` row with status PLACEHOLDER, never LOCKED) include `MAX_EMITTERS` (Emitter), `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` (Emitter), `LANTERN_LIGHTS_MAX`, `NONSHADOW_LIGHTS_MAX`, `SHADOW_SLOTS_MAX` and `OCCLUDER_POLYS_VIEW_MAX` (Lighting) and `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX` (Item DB), beside the rows already so marked in the text. Output is ordered by `(template_id, lint_id, record_key)` ascending and two runs over the same bytes produce identical bytes; waivers are rows `{lint_id, record_key, reason, owner}` in the same file, a waiver without a reason is an ERROR, and `frame_exception` and the PD-1 exceptions are waivers of this form (no waiver may name `STORY_SLOT_FILLED_IN_MVP`). -> ADR candidate: the lint runner's shape, language and location.

**Lint tranches (design review 2026-10-27).** The 90-row table *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up; Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: was 88, plus `DECISION_REACH_MISSING` and `DWELL_BUDGET_LOW`)* is delivered in tranches so CI can be turned on early: **T0** the six BOOT rules; **T1, the minimum CI set**, the ERROR rows that guard the shipped data (`AUTHORED_NON_INTEGER`, `SPAWN_BLOCKED`, `SELF_POWERED_LAMP_COUNT`, `BEACON_COUNT`, `RESIDUAL_LEDGER_CLOSED`, `FAULT_COUNT_EIGHT`, `ORPHAN_LOW_HP`, `POWER_TREE_MISSING`, `OVERLOAD_WINDOW`, `EMPTY_WINDOW`, `FROZEN_ROOM_UNFORCED`, `COLD_ROOM_UNDECLARED`, `COLD_WORK_BUDGET`, `RETURN_PATH_TRAVERSE`, `ESCAPE_ROUTE_OVER_BUDGET`, `FIXTURE_COUNT_ROOM`, `FIXTURE_COUNT_WORLD`, `TRAVERSE_TOO_LONG`, `TUNING_SOURCE_DRIFT`, `STORY_SLOT_FILLED_IN_MVP`); **T2** the remaining ERROR rows (`DWELL_BUDGET_LOW` is T2 and runs red on the current content plan until the plan adds dwell sources, D.23, OQ-DA-56; `DECISION_REACH_MISSING` joins T2 as an ERROR once the spike fixes the geometry); **T3** the WARN rows and the ASSET rows. A tranche is complete when its rows run clean on the three shipped templates, and a later tranche never blocks an earlier one *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

**DA-42 Content acceptance runs as headless tests, not lint.** Rules that need the Graph to run are integration tests in `tests/integration/derelict_authoring/` using the kernel's headless path (H10): `G0_ZERO_PART_SURVIVABLE` (IDB AC-42: a patch-only run of all eight faults ends none FAILED or DESTROYED, including the case "`v_o2_cryo` never opened", which passes because the isolated duct holds its `hp` (gated, DA-16), and the case "seam breaker closed onto a dead B" with its re-open recovery, `G0_SEAM_CLOSED_ON_DEAD_B`, DA-20) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, `G0_SCRUBBER_INSTALL` (a scrubber installed in a home slot with `j_cryo` closed, recovered by opening `j_cryo` and a zero-part PATCH, DA-47) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, `FAULT_D15_DRIFT` (each live fault within +-15 % of the Graph's D.15 at its trigger), `DEAD_FAULT_STILL_REPAIRABLE` (every dry dead fault is still repairable, at its authored `hp`, after `T_SORTIE_LOADED_MAX` minutes of loaded idle, because gated wear holds it; and the flowing-time ledger itself is asserted: `pb_leak` repairable at 15.0 min and DESTROYED at 15.3 min of flowing time counted from V12, with the reservoir topped up past step 2,112, where B's own 16,000 mL runs out, D.21) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, `WELD_WITHOUT_PLATE_SURVIVABLE` (welding B with no plate and patching only keeps `seg_b_pump_n` from staying below `BREACH_HP` for more than 60 s at a 3.5-minute patch cadence) and `SCRAM_RESTART_STEADY` (patch `pb_leak`, then V12: `r_b` reaches RUNNING and does not re-SCRAM in 10 minutes; skip the patch and the stock crosses the floor at step 1,238 after V12 and `r_b` SCRAMs again after it and by step 1,826, about 1,588 expected, D.21 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*). The harness shape is an ADR candidate; they are tagged (auto) in Acceptance Criteria.

**DA-43 The authoring workflow (data first; editor tooling deferred).** (1) The floor plan is drawn as an ASCII grid in `tools/asset-pipeline/plans/<template_id>.txt` (one character per 48 px tile, half-tile walls on the 24 px lattice per LY-3). (2) A converter turns the grid into `tile_runs`, wall runs and the rect table. (3) Placements, nodes, fixtures, faults and ledgers are hand-written records. (4) The offline lint runs Layout's structural rules, then this GDD's CONTENT rules. (5) The template is written to `assets/data/sections/`. **Plan grammar (minimum, normative until the converter ADR lands) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*:** one character per 48 px tile; a letter per `room_key`, as in the C.1 to C.3 drawings; `+` a door position on the wall run it sits in; `-` a corridor tile; `H` a socket hatch; a space is exterior; `x:` and `y` header rows carry tile indices; the 24 px wall strips and the hull runs are derived by the converter and never drawn. Jogs, hull-segment and plate-position marks and loose-slot marks belong to the converter ADR, and **that ADR is a prerequisite of the first template story**, because without it TEMPLATE_DRAFT to TEMPLATE_CONVERTED is unspecified. **Positions live in the template files:** every anchor, fixture, hardpoint, plate position, loose slot and route length is a record there, the figures in this GDD are design values, and acceptance criteria check "within +-48 px of the template value". **Proposed story split** (for `/create-stories`; the effort is XL): S1 plan grammar ADR and converter; S2 lint runner, tranches T0 and T1 (DA-41); S3 headless harness and the G-0 family; S4 `home_ship`; S5 `derelict_a`; S6 `derelict_b` and the weld; S7 kit manifest and the `_worn` atlas; S8 lint tranches T2 and T3; S9 playtest rounds X-DA-1 to X-DA-10. A Godot editor exporter (an `@tool` or EditorPlugin route) is deferred to an ADR and a spike. **Engine feasibility:** the pinned reference has no scripting, core, editor or tools module (`docs/engine-reference/godot/modules/` covers animation, audio, input, navigation, networking, physics, rendering and ui only), so every claim about JSON loading, editor tooling, TileMapLayer authoring and export filters is `[UNV]`: `JSON.parse_string` returning floats (cast and bound-check every authored integer), the export include filter for `assets/data/**/*.json` (a 30-minute spike against the 4.7.2 export templates, IDB OQ 23) and a 4.7 typed-return override rule for any loader or lint base class are the items to verify. -> ADR candidates: authoring tool pipeline and the offline lint runner; headless content harness; worn-atlas derivation tool; atlas selection by `template_id` in the PROJECTOR. This GDD describes behaviour and data shape, never code.

**DA-44 Template edits and registry discipline.** A template edit changes its `template_hash` (LY-26 drift policy); saves before the first external build are disposable (SL-14) and template migration begins only after it. A `room_key` change is committed only with a note in the file header. Registry discipline: owner-named values that are not registry entries (`ESCAPE_ROUTE_BUDGET_PX`, `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE`, `MAX_STORY_SLOTS_PER_SECTION`, `DOOR_CORNER_MARGIN_PX`, `TILES_WORLD_MVP`, `SOCKET_SPACING_MIN_TILES`) are cited by owner GDD and rule and never minted here; `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` (Interaction) and `part_cover_pm` (Item DB F-11, with its band as variables) are registry entries and are cited by name and registry value; `FREEZE_ON_MC` -20000 is a registry entry (Graph-owned), as are `LEAK_HP` and `BREACH_HP` 4000. The final stage registers only this GDD's own artefacts, from the final text: the template roster (`template_id` values) (registry: `mvp_template_roster`), the `visible_unknown` record, the MVP fixture-count table (citing LY D.16) (registry: `mvp_fixture_counts`), the fault-allocation table (registry: `mvp_fault_allocation`), the MVP ledger instance (citing IDB F-11) (registry: `mvp_part_ledger`), the legal loaded sets of DA-32 (registry: `legal_loaded_sets`), the content lint id list (registry: `content_lint_ids`), the kit-manifest schema name (registry: `kit_manifest_schema`) and the `_worn` token (registry: `worn_variant_token`); the 36 `pending referenced_by` comments become real `referenced_by` entries. (`RESERVE_S` 90 and `INSUL_MC` 5000 were registered by Suit & Equipment after this list was first drawn; they are cited as registry entries, with Suit's placeholder tags kept at each mention.)

**DA-45 The first ten minutes are content-shaped (MVP onboarding).** MVP onboarding is the cryo-bay fixture and a hand-led sequence the layout supports; there is no scripted tutorial and no marker. The player wakes with one guttering EMERGENCY lamp; closes the cryo breaker `j_cryo` (V3); the room warms at about 4 mC per Graph step gross (500 W into C = 42 x `C_TILE` 3000 = 126,000 dJ/°C, LSG D.7), about 3 mC net of hull loss at 13 exposed tiles, so about 6.5 minutes (about 3,900 Graph steps) from -2000 mC to COMFORT at 10000 mC; LSG:839's 2.5 minutes is its 20-tile example (D.18); follows the frost trail from `p_cryo_rad` to the failed fitting; patches it (V1). **V5 is first taught here** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `v_o2_cryo` stands shut on the wall beside `door_cryo_s`, in the same sweep as the door and clear of its approach square and of the coolant floor run, and the bay's own air with the door and the valve both shut reaches THIN at 7.0 minutes (D.19; the Graph's atmosphere descriptor, no new tell), a few minutes after the warm-up and the patch, so a player who lingers meets the thinning air with the valve in view. Opening it (V5) restores the bay's supply, starts the hiss at the bare duct and arms O2-SHIP, so the first valve a player turns is a lever with a price, and the answer to the price is the zero-part patch (D.19); a player who leaves through the door never needs it, which is the point of a lever. The cryo-bay door opens onto a lit corridor with the beacon dark; the engine-room door `door_engine_n` stands open (DA-16) and the ember glow of the Fuel Ember row at the `pf_feed` flange is the next thing in view. Later onboarding (Vertical Slice) replaces the hand lead with the Onboarding system. **The first sortie's dwell is content-shaped too** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, Boarding & Salvage D.1, D73)*: the concept's five-minute W_LOOP leaves about 190 s of discretionary dwell that the content, and nothing else, must produce on the way to `htr_a` and back (witnesses read per room, decision points, door choices, stick or lantern pauses); `DWELL_BUDGET_LOW` (D.23) holds the plan to `A_DWELL_BUDGET_MIN_S` 190 s, and nobody tunes the route length or `V_CARRY` to hit five minutes.

**DA-46 The dock status glow (user ruling X2 on D58 and D70a, 2026-10-27).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* Ruling: Web Map & Transit option (B). The docked pod's dock-room prop carries a small self-powered status glow that counts as an ordinary lit fixture and is not a fourth `act_gate` exception, so `pod_hop`'s lit gate is always satisfied at the console; Interaction's refusal of a `pod_hop` lit exemption (IA-6) stands. The Authoring side:

- *Placement.* Exactly one glow per dock room, on the 96 x 96 docked-podcraft prop (kit gap K-3, DA-6): `fx_air_pod` in `airlock`, `fx_a_dock_pod` in `a_dock`, `fx_b_dock_pod` in `b_dock`; none in `pod_cabin`. Each is `node_ref` 0 (no Graph LOAD, no draw and no clock: its source is the docked pod's own standby cell, a diegetic fact of the prop, so it cannot drain, be cut, or be served or unserved by the room's bus), `switch_on` true at build, not switchable by PLAYER or SYSTEM origin and never modulated by Attention's tell, as the EMERGENCY lamp is (LO-20). The class id, and whether the Emitter refuses `set_switch` on it, are the Emitter's amendment `[PLACEHOLDER: Emitter EM-3 / EM-11]`. It is not a residual light: it has no island, no battery and no ledger.
- *Lit at the console, dark at the arrival.* Two inequalities from Web Map D.15 over Emitter State's chain (D.1b: R = `DOCK_STATUS_REACH_PX` 48, c(d) = floor(`DOCK_STATUS_BASE_PM` x floor(1000 x (R - d) / R) / 1000), base 500) bind every dock room. **G1 (the console is lit by the glow alone):** each CONSOLE anchor in the room (the POD anchor in all three dock rooms, the DOCK anchor in the home airlock, OQ-DA-39) lies within `DOCK_STATUS_CONSOLE_MAX_PX` = 24 px of the glow origin (this value is owned HERE, as a placement lint; Emitter I-DOCK bounds it below the reach): c(24) = floor(500 x 500 / 1000) = 250 >= `LIT_ON_PM` 150 plus Emitter's margin. **G2 (the arrival cell stays dark, so a dark derelict stays dark):** c(d_arr) < `LIT_OFF_PM` 120 for d_arr = glow origin to the centre of the room's `arrive_*` cell, i.e. d_arr >= `DOCK_STATUS_ARR_MIN_PX` = 37 (c(36) = 125 fails, c(37) = 114 passes; derived by the lint from Emitter's values, never authored). The earlier 192 px cap (48 + 48 + 96) and the request that the Emitter light a point 192 px away are WITHDRAWN: 192 px exceeds the glow's reach of 48 and any glow that lit it would light the arrival cell. **Placement rule:** the glow sits on the docked-podcraft prop (96 x 96) at most 24 px from every CONSOLE anchor; if the arrival cell centre is r_arr >= 61 px from the console anchor G2 holds for any such placement (61 - 24 = 37); if r_arr < 61 (WM-15's nearest layout is 24 px) put the glow origin on the far side of the prop from the arrival cell, delta px beyond the console anchor on the line from the arrival cell, delta in [13, 24] (d_c = delta, d_arr = r_arr + delta; at r_arr = 24 that is 37 to 48, c from 114 down to 0, and G1 holds with c(13) = 364 down to c(24) = 250); nominal delta = 20 (c 291 at the console, c 41 at the arrival cell). The console stays within two tiles of `arrive_*` (OQ-DA-39) and within the player's reach. `DOCK_GLOW_LIT_AT_CONSOLE` and `DOCK_GLOW_ARRIVAL_DARK` assert the two headless statements (BS-19: lit at the console with the bus dead, every other emitter dark and the beam off; and dark at the arrival cell). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G1)*
- *Separation from the visible unknown (art-director).* The glow must never read as the exterior unknown: its `output_pm` is at most `DOCK_GLOW_OUTPUT_MAX_PM` (provisional 250 permille of the residual lamp's `output_pm`, `[PLACEHOLDER: art-director, derive at the art spike]`), its hue class is not the residual lamp's Tungsten and is outside the flesh and violet bands (DA-37), and no dock room carries a `wall_port` piece, so the glow cannot appear in the exterior transit view. `VISIBLE_UNKNOWN_COMPETITOR` is unchanged because a dock room is never a host room (`a_life`, `b_breach`); `WAKE_SINGLE_LIGHT` is scoped to `cryo_bay` and is unaffected.
- *Counts.* Read conservatively as fixtures (D.3): the dock rooms stand at 2 of 4 (`airlock`), 2 of 3 (`a_dock`) and 2 of 3 (`b_dock`); the sets stand at S0 9, S1 16, S2 and S3 17 and S4 24 against 28. Read as a separate Emitter class, the fixture counts stay 8, 14, 15, 15 and 21 and the glows enter only the emitter tally (S4: 21 + 3 + 8 + 1 + 9 = 42 against the Emitter's derived 70). Both readings fit, so the registry request on `mvp_fixture_counts` is conditional on the Emitter's classification.
- *Ownership of BS-19.* The invariant is owned jointly: Web Map & Transit and the Emitter guarantee the glow's behaviour and class; Authoring lints its placement (`DOCK_GLOW_MISSING`, `DOCK_GLOW_COUNT`, `DOCK_GLOW_LIT_AT_CONSOLE`, `DOCK_GLOW_ARRIVAL_DARK`, `DOCK_GLOW_LUMINANCE`, `POD_CONSOLE_MISSING`, `DOCK_CONSOLE_MISSING`); Interaction's IA-6 is unchanged. BS-19 holds in the content iff AC-DA-143 to AC-DA-145, AC-DA-157 and AC-DA-158 pass *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G1)*.
- *Reconciled rules.* DA-23 (the EMERGENCY lamp remains the only EMERGENCY `node_ref` 0 fixture), DA-27 (the second-self-powered-subkind rejection concerns the visible unknown), DA-37 and AC-V2 (the dock glow joins the closed list of chromatic sources in a dark wreck) and the Cross-References EM-11 row are amended to match.

**DA-47 The home scrubber slots: a content route for the carried scrubber (user ruling D72, 2026-10-27).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* Ruling: commit the content route now. `home_ship` authors two vacant scrubber hardpoints, `hp_cargo_scrubber` (`cargo_bay`, beside `scr_main`) and `hp_eng_scrubber` (`engine_room`): kind SCRUBBER (20), `socket_tag` `scrubber`, `accepted_subtypes` the scrubber def, `compatible_defs` at least 1, **no node** (vacant like `hp_cargo_heater`, so the node ledger, LOAD count and `NODE_COUNT_DRIFT` do not move). The exact anchor pixels are template data (acceptance is within 48 px, DA-29). **Why two.** Two is the least that makes the choice of where real; `MAX_HARDPOINTS_PER_SECTION` 32 leaves 3 spare at 29 (a third would leave 2); and every slot is one more declared interactable in every set containing `home_ship` (D.17, +2 here). A third is a data edit. **What competes.** The slots compete for the one scrubber the MVP guarantees (`scr_a`; `scr_b` is the weld donor's own air) and for the trunk's headroom, and the chosen slot decides which compartment's duct neighbourhood the new scrubber serves; the per-slot effect on the D.19 air clocks is a Graph computation `[PLACEHOLDER — derive at spike with the Graph owner]`. Consequences:

- `scr_a` now has a home destination: carrying it is a decision and no longer a payoff-less carry, and the MVP loop pays off more than once. G-0 is untouched (the slots are optional; the MVP stays survivable with zero parts). Boarding & Salvage amends BS-17 and the strip-the-wreck tests X-BS-9 return with it.
- **D.9 is re-solved, and the window is unchanged.** `OVERLOAD_WINDOW` is computed on the heater trigger over the published tree; a vacant slot adds 0 W, so the standing load stays 1880 W (1900 W with the beacon), `cap` 2161 W stays inside the legal 2159 to 2163 W, and AC-DA-28 still passes. What the slot adds is a second, player-made way into the same fault, tabulated here with `cap` 2161 W, `S` 2500 W and `dhp_ovl = floor(4 x (rho_pm - 1000)^2 / 10^4)`:

| Install state (one running 600 W scrubber added) | `L_below` (W) | `rho_pm` | `dhp_ovl` (bp per step) | Steps to DESTROYED |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Wake state, `j_cryo` open | 1240 + 600 = 1840 | 851 | 0 | none |
| `j_cryo` closed, no heater, beacon off | 1880 + 600 = 2480 | 1147 | 8 | 1,250 (2.1 min) |
| as above with the beacon lit | 1900 + 600 = 2500 | 1156 | 9 | 1,112 |
| `j_cryo` closed with the heater of `htr_a` also installed | 2380 + 600 = 2980, clamped to `S` 2500 | 1156 | 9 | 1,112 |
| as the second row with all 7 lamps switched off | 2480 - 140 = 2340 | 1082 | 2 | 5,000 |

  Reading it: lamps alone cannot defuse the second row (the trunk needs L at most 2161, 319 W less, and 7 lamps are 140 W); the defusing lever is opening `j_cryo`, which removes 640 W and returns 1840 W (rho 851). At 8 bp per step (beacon off) the trunk is DEGRADED at step 376, FAILED at 875 and DESTROYED at 1,250; at 9 (beacon lit, or the heater also installed) at 334, 778 and 1,112. The table holds before the weld and on the welded body while the seam breaker is open or `r_b` is SCRAMmed; with `r_b` running the pooled supply defuses it (DA-20). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*
- **Design decision (re-review, 2026-10-27; creative-director position, mirroring DA-20 option (b)): an install that overruns the trunk is a taught order with a stated recovery, not a silent run-ender.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* User ruling D72 (commit the slots) is not reopened; this settles its consequence. Options weighed: **(a) raise `cab_trunk` `cap`: struck**, because any `cap` of 2,480 W or more (the scrubber row) erases fault #3, whose legal window is 2159 to 2163 W (D.9); **(b) the taught order with a recovery window: adopted as the default**, because Authoring can close it alone; **(c) an install interlock** (`install_module` refuses, or the slot's power tie stays open, while the trunk headroom is below the module's `req_w`): a Graph and Assembly change, routed now as the fallback (OQ-DA-47). (i) *Precursor:* the headroom is a published fact (DA-20: 281 W, 261 W with the beacon lit), and the order is taught by the gentler case first: the reference first sortie carries `htr_a` (DA-48, Boarding & Salvage BS-17), whose install with `j_cryo` closed is fault #3 at 4 bp per step (DEGRADED at 75 s, DESTROYED at 4.2 minutes, the lamps-off lever works), so the player meets the hum, the dimming lamps and the warming box at `j_main` before they bring a scrubber home; `scr_a` is the second carry. (ii) *Live tell:* OVERLOAD sev 1 from the first step over (LSG mode 7), every home lamp dimming through the served ratio, the scorch at `j_main`. (iii) *Recovery window (`G0_SCRUBBER_INSTALL`, AC-DA-150):* the shed is opening `j_cryo` (V3; back to 1840 W, rho 851, no damage) or taking the scrubber out again. Shed before step 376 (334 with the beacon lit) and the trunk is never DEGRADED; before step 875 (778) it is DEGRADED with `hp` above 3,000 and a zero-part PATCH restores it to `PATCH_CAP_BP`; from 875 to 1,249 (778 to 1,111) it is FAILED, a home blackout, still repairable by a zero-part PATCH once the load is off; from step 1,250 (1,112) it is DESTROYED and `NOT_REPAIRABLE`. That is the one residual run-ender: it costs 125 s (111 s) of ignored tells, it is not a death, and its remedy is the last autosave (whether a module install autosaves is asked of Save/Load, OQ-DA-47). (iv) *Fallback:* if X-DA-11 shows more than 1 of 5 testers lose the trunk to an install, interlock (c) is built. The order the content teaches is **open the cryo branch, then install, and keep it open while the scrubber runs**: the shed is the cryo bay's heater, lamp and door motor (640 W), a real trade, which the welded body with `r_b` running lifts (DA-20). **The FAILED scrubber's power request is a decided default, not `[UNV]`:** an installed module requests its def `req_w` whatever its `state` (the Graph's STARVE rule keys on `req_w` > 0 with no state gate, LSG mode 6), so a FAILED `scr_a` (`hp` 2000) loads the trunk from the install step and makes no O2 until a PATCH raises it to `PATCH_CAP_BP` 6000; the cost therefore precedes the benefit, and the recovery window is counted from the install. The Graph confirms or overrides this default (OQ-DA-47); if it publishes a FAILED-draws-nothing rule, the window is counted from the patch and every figure stands.
- **Per-module install ledger (home trunk, `cap` 2161 W, `S` 2500 W, before the weld)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*:

| Module carried home (slot) | `req_w` | Over the headroom (beacon off / lit) | What must be shed to stay undamaged | What is gained |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `htr_a` (`hp_cargo_heater`) | 500 W | +219 / +239 W: 2380 / 2400 W, rho 1101 / 1110, 4 bp per step (fault #3: DEGRADED 751, FAILED 1,750, DESTROYED 2,500) | 6 lamps (7 with the beacon lit) to reach 2260 W, rho 1045, 0 bp per step (the hum stays); or the cryo branch (`j_cryo` open: 1740 W, rho 805) | heat into `cargo_bay`, which no authored room needs; its authored role is the fault #3 trigger and the reference carry, and it is a replacement body should `htr_cryo` wear out (duty wear, LS-25) |
| `scr_a` (`hp_cargo_scrubber` or `hp_eng_scrubber`) | 600 W (requested from install, even FAILED) | +319 / +339 W: 2480 / 2500 W, rho 1147 / 1156, 8 / 9 bp per step (DEGRADED 376 / 334, FAILED 875 / 778, DESTROYED 1,250 / 1,112) | lamps cannot (all 7 off leaves 2 / 3 bp per step); the cryo branch must be open (1840 / 1860 W, rho 851 / 860) for as long as the scrubber runs | once patched to run, +`R_SCR` 400 mGU per step of O2 make-up on the slot's duct network; with `scr_main` served the two scrubbers' 800 mGU per step meet the O2-SHIP drain at its worst (leak 500 + `VENT_HOLD` 300, D.19), so the cryo pool never drains while both run `[UNV: Graph duct tie and per-slot share, OQ-DA-47]` |
| both | 1100 W | +819 / +839 W: supply-clamped, rho 1156, 9 bp per step whatever the lamps | the cryo branch open **and** 4 lamps off (5 with the beacon lit): 2260 W, rho 1045, 0 bp per step; with the branch open and every lamp on, 2340 / 2360 W at 2 / 3 bp per step (DESTROYED 5,000 / 3,334) | both of the above; on the welded body with `r_b` running nothing need be shed (pooled 4220 W, rho 976, DA-20) |

- The slot's attachment (the O2 duct tie and the power tie below `j_main` that `install_module` makes, MSA-26 and MSA-27) is Assembly's and the Graph's; this file authors the slot, not the edges (OQ-DA-47). The D.17 count, the hardpoint count (DA-12), the overview table and AC-DA-13 are updated for the two slots.

**DA-48 The reference carry is 21 to 28 tiles (user ruling X3 on D71, 2026-10-27; geometry `[PLACEHOLDER — derive at spike]`).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* Ruling: Boarding option B. Attention adds a legible carry intake while a salvage carry is held (class-blind, any class and any mass, scoped to a carry picked up in a non-home locus body; Attention AT-3 BURDEN row, F-26, `U_BURDEN` registry `carry_burden_intake`, delegated ruling R5) and recalibrates its weights and theta; *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: the intake is class-blind, Attention owns it, G1; the stowed-HEAVY run-disable rule D18 and the `part_hull_plate` HEAVY wording are a different rule and stay)* the decision-bearing carry in the reference derelict is lengthened to about 21 to 28 tiles; freezing after the cut is priced by that intake. The Authoring side is the geometry of derelict A (the reference first sortie cuts `htr_a` in `a_machine` and carries it to the pod in `a_dock`, Boarding & Salvage BS-17). **Plan of record for the carries** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: `htr_a` is the first sortie's reference carry (this rule, REF_GOLDEN, AC-DA-148) and `scr_a` is the second carry (the leave-it-dark cut of the anchor moment, and the DA-47 install); the carry each playtest target assumes is listed in Game Feel (Playtest-checkable targets). **Plan-of-record switch:** until the spike confirms the lever (OQ-DA-45), C.2 and every AC that reads A's geometry use today's plan (13 tiles), and the DA-48 lever is a proposal; the switch flips C.2, D.1, D.18, the door table and AC-DA-148 together, in one edit. **Constraint.** `REF_CARRY_TILES_MIN` 21 and `REF_CARRY_TILES_MAX` 28 (1,008 to 1,344 px, which is 1,008 to 1,344 ticks at `V_CARRY` 1, 16.8 to 22.4 s one way; the W_LOOP dwell budget of about 200 s of D73 absorbs it), with at most 7 tiles (336 px) inside the lethal cold room (DA-28). **Today's plan delivers 13 tiles.** Boarding's figure is 624 ticks (cited, not edited here); it decomposes, from the door centres of C.2, as the in-room leg 204 px (`htr_a` to the centre of `door_a_spine_machine`, (420, 504)) + the door-to-door leg 312 px (to the centre of `door_a_dock_spine`, (252, 360): 168 + 144) + the dock leg 108 px (to `arrive_a_dock`, (168, 336): 84 + 24) = 624 px = 13 tiles. **Why the doors cannot be moved to fix it.** Each of the two doors sits at the only legal place in its shared wall run (a 240 px straight run holds exactly one 192 px assembly plus the 48 px corner margin, DA-8: `door_a_dock_spine` start 312 and `door_a_spine_machine` start 456 are forced), and placing `htr_a` at the room's far wall adds at most 132 px (336 - 204), which still leaves 756 px at 13 + 2.75 = 15.75 tiles. **Candidate lever (provisional).** (L1) move `a_dock` to the north end of `a_spine` (origin (48, 96); its door start is forced to 192, centre (252, 240)), which lengthens the door-to-door leg from 312 to 432 px (168 + 264), +120 px; (L2) lengthen `a_spine` by n tiles and shift `a_machine` south by n tiles, +48n px. Then R(n, d) = (204 + d) + 432 + 48n + 108 = 744 + 48n + d px, where d = `REF_CARRY_CUT_OFFSET_PX` is how much farther from the centre of `door_a_spine_machine` the cut point (the `htr_a` anchor) sits inside `a_machine`; the in-room leg 204 + d must stay at most 336 px (DA-28 cap), so 0 <= d <= 132 (the far-wall headroom already stated above). Inside the band for n = 6 to 12 at d = 0. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* **The binding constraint is the cold-room drift of the longer spine (D.18).** With V = 33 + 3n and S_ext = 11 + 2n + 1 (the enlarged east gap counted as n + 1 tiles, the conservative reading) at the current `T0` -12000 mC, n = 6 gives tau = 12000 x 51 / 24 = 25,500 and t = 25,500 x ln(1.8) / 600 = 24.98 minutes, below `COLD_ROOM_SAFE_MIN` 25, so the current `T0` fails. Warming `a_spine` to -10000 mC (the same as `a_life`; not below `FREEZE_WARN_MC`) gives t = tau x ln(2) / 600: n = 6, 29.5 minutes; n = 9 (V 60, S_ext 30, tau 24,000), **27.7 minutes**; n = 12 (V 69, S_ext 36, tau 23,000), 26.6 minutes: all pass. **Provisional design value (amended 2026-10-28): n = 12 and d = 24**, R = 744 + 48 x 12 + 24 = 744 + 576 + 24 = 1,344 px = **28.0 tiles** (22.4 s one way at `V_CARRY` 1; in-room leg 204 + 24 = 228 px, 108 px under the 336 px cap), `a_spine` 3 x 23 (V = 33 + 3 x 12 = 69, S_ext = 11 + 2 x 12 + 1 = 36, tau = 12000 x 69 / 36 = 23,000, t = 23,000 x ln(2) / 600 = 26.6 minutes at `T0` -10000 mC; already shown above to pass `COLD_ROOM_SAFE_MIN` 25), `a_dock` at (48, 96) unchanged, `a_machine` origin (432, 792 + 3 x 48) = (432, 936) (base y 360 + 48n: 792 at n = 9, 936 at n = 12), the `htr_a` anchor 24 px farther from the door centre inside `a_machine` (for example (600, 480) to (624, 480); verify against the room wall and hardpoint spacing with Layout's converter), A at `size_tiles` 17 x 26 (14 + 12; n = 9 was 17 x 23), A's V total 145 + 3 x (12 - 9) = 154, S1 327 + 9 = 336 and S4 476 + 9 = 485 of `TILES_WORLD_MVP` 600 (below the WARN at 540; first-order, three spine tiles per added row; Layout's converter confirms). **Why n = 12 with d = 24:** the K3 live share is live_pm = floor((Lc - t_x - 900) x 1000 / 900) with t_x = 318, 336, 360, 384 for the lit pre-lift dwells 0, 300, 900, 1800 (Boarding D.2 K3), and any share needs Lc >= 1,219. Sweep at d = 0: n = 9 gives Lc 1,176 and 0/0/0/0; n = 10 gives 1,224 and 6/0/0/0; n = 11 gives 1,272 and 60/40/13/0 (minimum 0); n = 12 gives 1,320 and 113/93/66/40 (minimum 40); n = 12 with d = 24 gives 1,344 and 140/120/93/66 (minimum 66), the figures Boarding and Attention F-26 already quote, so no Attention figure changes. A reference carry under 1,219 px is a live-share defect that Attention IR-4 already reports as `ATT_CARRY_NOT_LIVE`; no new lint id is added. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* These are placeholders: the C.2 tables, the ASCII plan, D.1, D.18, the door table and Layout's `exposed_tiles` still describe today's plan until the spike confirms the lever with Layout's converter, so every C.2 figure above is the pre-ruling plan and `REF_CARRY_LENGTH` stays a WARN, not an ERROR (OQ-DA-45). Lint `REF_CARRY_LENGTH` and AC-DA-148 carry the band; the Attention intake and the REF_GOLDEN margins of AC-BS-123 are Attention's and Boarding's to re-derive from the new length.

*(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: counterpart text for Boarding & Salvage ASK-BS-D4 (d), (e) and (f), G6; the requirement is Boarding's ruled behaviour, X3 and D71, and is the spec; the geometry below stays `[PLACEHOLDER — derive at spike]` with the rest of this rule)* **Decision reach (ASK-BS-D4 (d)).** The lit tell opens `t_x` ticks into the carry, 318, 336, 360 and 384 for the lit pre-lift dwells 0, 300, 900 and 1,800 (Boarding D.2 K3), and the carry is measured from the cut point, so with the in-room leg at 228 px (n = 12, d = 24) the onset falls **90, 108, 132 and 156 px past the centre of `door_a_spine_machine`** (318 - 228, 336 - 228, 360 - 228, 384 - 228). Boarding also asks the reach to cover the dark variant (onset at 252 px) and an earlier onset of 54 px if the built route's walk-in lowers `t_x` (EC-BS-68). `a_spine` therefore provides, over the route-distance window **54 to 252 px past the centre of `door_a_spine_machine`** (`DECISION_REACH_NEAR_PX` 54, `DECISION_REACH_FAR_PX` 252; 198 px, 4.1 tiles; if `REF_CARRY_CUT_OFFSET_PX` d is raised to 48 the in-room leg is 252 px and every onset falls 24 px earlier, still inside the window): (1) a floor reach **at least 3 tiles (144 px) wide** (`a_spine` is exactly 3 tiles wide, so the whole corridor width, with no prop, shelf or fitting inside the window that narrows it below 144 px); (2) **no door cell**: no cell of any door assembly (`door_a_spine_machine`, `door_a_dock_spine`, `door_a_spine_life`) lies inside the window, because a `drop` never lands on a door cell (Interaction `drop_placement`) and a tell that opens in a doorway leaves only the worse answers (EC-BS-68); at the proposal the dock door is about 1,008 px of route from the machine door, so the window is clear of it, whereas today's 13-tile plan (door-to-door 312 px) puts `door_a_dock_spine` inside it; (3) **lit or with a stick shelf**: the window lies inside the lit pool (`r_off`) of an authored `a_spine` fixture (`fx_a_spine_2`, placed at the spike so its pool covers the window centre; both `a_spine` fixtures are dark at build and light when the bus is woken, DA-13, so before the wake the reach is lit by the carrier's own beam, which is the honest restraint option for Attention), and no new loose slot is authored in this pass, so the seeded counts and the glowstick total of DA-26 do not change (a stick shelf in the reach is the alternative, OQ-DA-56); (4) **outside the open-door cold projection, or its cost printed beside it (D.18)**: opening `door_a_spine_machine` mixes `a_spine` toward `a_machine` by `K_OPEN` conduction, so the window sits at the door and inside the projection by construction; `DECISION_REACH_MISSING` prints the projected `T` of the window cells with the door open and the cost (minutes to the shifted onset) beside the closed-door row, and the reach stays outside the lethal-cold cap of 7 tiles (336 px) inside `a_machine` because it is outside the room. Lint `DECISION_REACH_MISSING` (DA-40; WARN until the spike confirms the lever, then ERROR). **The 600 px question (ASK-BS-D4 (e); also DA-29).** `ESCAPE_ROUTE_BUDGET_PX` 600 carrying binds the route from an endogenously failing anchor to a closable door, and `ESCAPE_ROUTE_CARRY` applies only to a room where a module node shares the room with a live fault; neither binds the 1,008 to 1,344 px decision carry (the carry is a chosen act at the player's pace, not an escape from a gated failure), so **no exemption is granted or needed**, and a lint that rejects the ruled route fails AC-BS-52 (AC-DA-161). **The `a_spine` junction (ASK-BS-D4 (f); X-BS-13, X-BS-14), re-specified against the 3 x 23 spine at n = 12.** With L1 the dock is at the north end (origin (48, 96)) and the three doors meet the spine at its north end: `door_a_dock_spine` (west wall, start 192, leaf [192, 288), centre (252, 240)) faces `door_a_spine_life` (east wall, start 192, leaf [192, 288), centre (420, 240), OPEN, unchanged), so a player stepping out of the dock sees straight across the spine into `a_life`, where the residual lamp `lamp_a_life` burns (DA-27). That is **the single leading element and it pulls toward `a_life` and `scr_a`, away from the reference payoff** (X-BS-14); the way to `a_machine` is the spine's southbound run, 840 px of corridor from y 240 to y 1,080 with `door_a_spine_machine` at its far end (start 1,032, leaf [1,032, 1,128), centre (420, 1,080), assembly [936, 1,128); both numbers are the proposal's 456 + 48 x 12 shift, CLOSED) and its frost fan creeping over the sill, which the beam shows only inside its reach. No marker, hint or second leading element is added (G-5, G-7, P1); the `part_hull_plate` on the `a_spine` floor is the one authored object on the southbound run, to be placed on it at the spike (so the long run is not empty) and not within 1 tile of a fault's floor run. The door table and the ASCII plan of C.2 flip to these values together with the plan-of-record switch (OQ-DA-45): `door_a_dock_spine` x [240, 264), y [96, 336), start 192, assembly [96, 288); `door_a_spine_life` unchanged; `door_a_spine_machine` x [408, 432), y [936, 1,176), start 1,032, assembly [936, 1,128). Routes: dock door to machine door 168 + 840 = 1,008 px (the 432 px of L1 plus 576 px), which is the 1,344 px reference carry of R(12, 24).



### States and Transitions

Authoring has **no runtime state** (DA-1): nothing here is a state block and nothing is hashed by Authoring. The states below are (1) the lifecycle of a template as a content artefact and (2) the states of authored subjects whose *initial* value this GDD writes and whose *transitions* belong to the owner named in the last column. The point of listing them is to say who may cause each change and to make every authored initial state one of the values the owner already defines.

**Template lifecycle (content states).**

| State | Entry condition | Exit condition | Transitions | Who may cause them |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TEMPLATE_DRAFT` | a plan file `tools/asset-pipeline/plans/<template_id>.txt` exists, or any edit to a shipped template | the converter runs without error | to `TEMPLATE_CONVERTED` | the level-designer (author) |
| `TEMPLATE_CONVERTED` | `tile_runs`, wall runs and the rect table are produced | Layout's structural lint reports zero ERROR | to `TEMPLATE_LINTED`; back to `TEMPLATE_DRAFT` on any ERROR | the converter; Layout's lint (CI) |
| `TEMPLATE_LINTED` | the structural lint passes | the full CONTENT lint (DA-40) reports zero ERROR | to `TEMPLATE_CONTENT_CLEAN`; back to draft on any ERROR | the CONTENT lint (CI) |
| `TEMPLATE_CONTENT_CLEAN` | the CONTENT lint passes (WARNs listed, not blocking) | the six-rule BOOT subset (DA-41) passes inside a build | to `TEMPLATE_SHIPPED` | the build / CI |
| `TEMPLATE_SHIPPED` | the file is in `assets/data/sections/` and its `template_hash` is recorded | any edit | to `TEMPLATE_DRAFT` (new `template_hash`; saves before the first external build are disposable, SL-14) | the author only; never at runtime (LY-2) |

**Authored subjects (initial state written here; transitions owned elsewhere).**

| Subject | State | Entry condition | Exit condition | Transitions | Who may cause them |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Fault (live or dead) | `LATENT` | held by a lever at build: a closed valve (flow only, DA-16), an open breaker, a thin margin, an absent load, or an absent weld | the lever is released | to `ACTIVE` when the player or the weld releases it; PWR-OVL on installing a heater; O2-ROOM on the weld | the player (V3, V5, install, V9); Assembly (the weld) |
| Fault | `DRIED` | unserved at build, `intensity_pm` 0, `since_steps` at least `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` `[PLACEHOLDER: Failure & Trail]` | power or coolant reaches it | to `ACTIVE`; a non-leaky dead fault is otherwise stable, a leaky one wears (DA-17) | the player feeding the branch; the Graph's wear |
| Fault | `ACTIVE` | `intensity_pm` above 0; joins the glow count | the Graph's mode machine | to the Graph's DEGRADED, FAILED, DESTROYED, PATCHED states (the Graph's, not Authoring's) | the Graph (endogenous); the player (patch, repair, close a valve) |
| Residual lamp | `LIT` | island battery stock above 0, lamp `switch_on` true | stock reaches 0 | to `DRAINED` while the section is loaded; the lamp dims for one step and goes dark, it never fades for effect | the Graph (D.6 draw); a Suit top-up withdrawal shortens it step for step |
| Residual lamp | `DRAINED` | battery stock 0 | the tie is closed on a powered bus | to `CHARGING`; otherwise none | the player closing the tie junction (V3) with the bus served |
| Residual lamp | `CHARGING` | tie closed, bus served by a source (B only; on A the tie merges the batteries, D.16, and `NO_SOURCE` applies) | stock at its cap, or the tie opens | back to `LIT` as stock rises; recharge at `min(S - served, CH_CAP)` per step (D.6) | the player; the Graph |
| Wreck bus (A, B) | `DEAD` | build state: junction open or broken (A), reactor SCRAMmed (B) | the junction is replaced or patched and closed (A); V12 restarts the reactor (B) | to `WOKEN`: lanterns come on, dormant faults emit, the DERELICT clock applies to the loaded section | the player (swap breaker, V12, V3) |
| Door | `CLOSED` / `OPEN` | `area` 0 or `area_max` at build per the room tables | the player or an opening event | between the two (the Graph's `area` and `door_state`, Layout's leaf presentation); `cut` and `welded` leaf states are not used on interior doors in MVP (DA-8) | Interaction (`set_opening_area`, M13); the Graph |
| Socket | `FREE` | build state (the only MVP sockets are `sock_airlock_e`, `sock_b_w`) | the weld | `FREE` to `WELDED` to `CAPPED` or `CUT_OPEN`, never back (Layout's `socket_state`) | Assembly's atomic apply, through Layout's LM2 `attach_section` and LM3 `detach_section` (Assembly is on disk, In Design) |
| Section load | `LOADED` / `DORMANT` | built and loaded / not loaded | load or unload | the Graph skips a dormant section's slots (LS-17); `can_join` returns `SECTION_NOT_LOADED` | Web Map & Transit (provisional — undesigned); not Authoring |
| Loose item | `PLACED` | seeded by `place_loose` at world build | picked up | to carried (Interaction's holder) and back by a drop | Interaction; Authoring owns only the initial placement |
| Module node | at its authored `hp` | built | cut free, installed, patched, repaired, destroyed | the Graph and Interaction's modules lifecycle (M6, M7, M5) | the player; the Graph |

A section's template state, a body's pose and a fault's mode are three different things: this GDD writes the first and the *initial* value of the last, and owns neither a transition nor a hash.


### Interactions with Other Systems

**Authoring registers no hook, no state block, no mutator and no read_view (DA-1).** Every interface below is the consumer's or the provider's; `data in` means what Authoring reads to write or lint its files, `data out` means what Authoring's files supply. Disk status (Glob of `design/gdd/`, 2026-10-26, at the time this part was written): designed — kernel, Graph, Item DB, Controller, Pipeline, Emitter, 2D Lighting, Failure & Trail, Save/Load, Suit, Interaction, Ship Section & Layout, Ship Body & Interior Physics, Attention; **on disk, In Design (refreshed 2026-10-27)** — Modular Ship Assembly (159 KB; it names this GDD 14 times and its C11 flags the Assembly row below as wrong on ownership, now corrected); **on disk since 2026-10-26 (rows refreshed 2026-10-27 where an ask arrived)** — Boarding & Salvage, Web Map & Transit, Horror Entities, Sanity, Perception Filter, In-World Instruments, Chart Table, Adaptive Audio, HUD, Debug Overlay; **no file** — Procedural Derelict Generation, Environmental Storytelling, Onboarding. Every contract with a system in the last group is marked `(provisional — X undesigned/in flight)` and says what that GDD must repeat.

#### Upstream (this GDD depends on them)

| System (status) | Data in | Data out | Interface owner and exact names | Their own Interactions row, quoted (bidirectional check) |
|---|---|---|---|---|

Authoring: CONTENT and visual-rule lint, the authored files \| provisional — Derelict Authoring undesigned". **Consistent.** This GDD supplies every item on the right of that row and adds five asks to Layout (the hardpoint raise, OQ-DA-7, is done): a `pod` kind (OQ-DA-17), a port rect that is SOLID but not `OCCLUDES_LIGHT` (OQ-DA-19), cross-room edge anchors (OQ-DA-5), the converter's governing `exposed_tiles` (OQ-DA-13) and an expected integer for `route_length_px` (LY OQ-20; this GDD supplies estimates only) |

| **Web Map & Transit** (on disk; dependent, hard) | none (reads) | a stable `template_id` roster, `entry_room_key`, the `visible_unknown` records, `mvp_world.json`, the spawn table | Transit owns `ARRIVING`, the loaded set, the pod leg and the exterior view; it calls `init_world`, `build_section` x4 (`mvp_world.json` lists the `pod` template), Graph M1, Emitter E1 and Interaction `sync_section`, then the PROJECTOR (provisional — Web Map & Transit undesigned) | the reachable loaded sets of DA-32 (S4 in particular); a derelict left behind goes dormant when the pod leaves, never discarded; the exterior state rule (lit iff the lamp node is OK or DEGRADED and persisted stock is above 0; `residual_lamp_predicate`); the pod is one persistent Layout body built at `init_world` (`pod_body`) |
| **Procedural Derelict Generation** (no file; VS-A, hard) | none | the kit manifest, the record shapes, the lint table (DA-38 to DA-40) | Procgen assembles; it owns the grammar, seeding and chunk assembly | it consumes this GDD's manifest rows and lint rows as its rule set and must not fork them; VS-A junk placement keeps `T_home` >= 20 x `C_check` (3120 ticks; Boarding & Salvage C-BS-11) |
| **Environmental Storytelling Placement** (no file; VS, soft) | none | the reserved empty story-slot list and the three roles | Storytelling fills slots | it fills `story_slots` only in templates released to Vertical Slice, and flips `STORY_SLOT_FILLED_IN_MVP` for them |
| **Modular Ship Assembly** (on disk, In Design; neighbour) | none | socket records (lanes, tag, class), plate positions, the staged PWR-OVL install slot `hp_cargo_heater` | Assembly owns the weld, cut and install verbs, weld quality `q` and the MSA-11 host rule; `socket_plan`, LM2 and LM3 are Layout's; the atomic apply and its call order are Assembly's; `can_join` stays the Graph's, and the cap plate is Layout's presentation of CAPPED | the lane set POWER 0 + O2 0 + hatch and the offset (696, 384) (MSA-11 repeats both). Answers to MSA: G4, LS-26's "<= 12 compartments" bounds the welded body (home 5 + B 5 = 10), never the loaded set S4 (14), because A is never welded (DA-2; the Graph confirms, OQ-DA-32); G6(a), the first seam-breaker close is worked in D.9 (supply-clamped, rho 1156 on a SCRAMmed `r_b`; OQ-DA-34); G6(d), `j_main` and `j_b_main` are `mod_breaker_junction` with three 204 ports of `cap` 3000 (IDB AC-79), and the bare duct junctions' `cap` stays open (OQ-DA-33) |
| **Horror Entities** (designed; neighbour) | none | `room_adjacency`, `route_length_px`, emitter placements; **no entity placement** | Horror owns `HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX` 2 `[PLACEHOLDER: Horror]` Emitter HORROR subjects and pathing; Interaction's H in D.17 is `HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX` 2 (registry `subject_budget`) | authoring places no entity; the residual and EMERGENCY lamps are never modulated by the tell |
| **Sanity and Perception Filter** (on disk; neighbours) | none | truth-only data; the falsifier map (DA-34); no hallucination record | the Filter corrupts the module-body channel at runtime | it must not fabricate or erase the residual light in the exterior transit view; LY OQ-13's truth-channel request |
| **In-World Instruments & Inspect View** (on disk; neighbour) | none | the instrument placement record (IW-31); subject anchors through Layout; the DA-34 witnesses | Instruments owns the instrument records (`assets/data/instruments/`), the dial and the card; Authoring owns placement and the lints `INSTRUMENT_NO_BINDING`, `PATCH_OVER_OVERRIDE`, `LIE_SURFACE_FLOOR` | the placement record shape (IW-31); ship gauges are Instruments' surfaces, not the suit's or the HUD's (K-4); the instrument rows wait on OQ-DA-38 |
| **Onboarding (Cryo Bay)** (no file; VS) | none | the cryo bay as the fixture (DA-45) | Onboarding owns scripted teaching | MVP onboarding is hand-led and content-shaped; no authored marker |
| **Emitter, Web Map & Transit, Interaction, Boarding & Salvage, Modular Ship Assembly: user rulings X2, X3, D37, D72** (2026-10-27) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | the Emitter's self-powered dock-glow class; Interaction's adjacency registration; Attention's carry intake | the three dock status glow placements and their lints (DA-46); the two vacant scrubber slots (DA-47); the reference carry route and band (DA-48) | the Emitter owns the class (an EM-3 / EM-11 amendment is owed); Web Map & Transit owns the console and `pod_hop`; Interaction owns IA-6 (unchanged) and the registration predicate (IA-37); Assembly owns `install_module` (MSA-26, MSA-27); Boarding & Salvage owns BS-19 and the carry sequence | the glow is not a fourth `act_gate` exception; BS-19 is satisfied jointly; the reference carry band is 21 to 28 tiles; `scr_a` has a home destination |
| **Adaptive Audio, HUD, Chart Table** (on disk) | none | authored objects only | their own GDDs | this GDD authors no audio rule and names no HUD shape; the dev lint report is plain text in the dev console |


## Formulas

**Conventions.** Authoring has no runtime math of its own (DA-1): every formula below is either a **lint formula** (a pure function of the authored files plus `content_lint_config`, DA-41), a **derivation** that fixes an authored number from a Graph, Item DB or Layout formula (so the number is solved, not guessed), or the **closed form a headless test asserts** (DA-42). All inputs are authored integers. A Graph step is `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6 ticks = 0.1 s, so 600 steps = 1 minute; watts are dJ per step (LSG LS-1). Formula ids `D.1` to `D.22` are fixed by the index in Core Rules and are never renumbered; the Graph's formulas are always written `LSG D.n` (the Graph's own D.15 is the fixture table, this GDD's D.15 is the patch and fatigue treadmill). Nothing here is hashed or replicated; the only transcendental function (D.18's natural logarithm) is lint-side and forbidden on the sim path (LSG LS-1 g), and the lint evaluates it with a fixed-point table so two runs over the same bytes print identical bytes (DA-41). Values resting on a provisional upstream carry `[PLACEHOLDER: owner]` at every mention.

### D.1 Tile volume budget per reachable set

The `tile_volume_budget` formula is defined as:

`world_tiles(S) = sum over every room r of every section in S of V(r) + POD_TILES x pods_loaded(S)` ; `ERROR iff world_tiles(S) > TILES_WORLD_MVP` ; `WARN iff 100 x world_tiles(S) >= WORLD_TILES_WARN_PCT x TILES_WORLD_MVP`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `V(r)` | int tiles | 4 to 200 | LY D.3 (`room_volume`) | Volume of one compartment; the room tables of C.1 to C.3 are the authored values |
| `S` | set of sections | the five reachable sets of DA-32 | DA-32 `[provisional: Web Map & Transit undesigned]` | The sections loaded at the same time |
| `POD_TILES` | int tiles | 4 | DA-3 (`pod_cabin` `V` 4) | Added when the persistent pod body exists (always dormant, WM-5); a pod is not in the three-ship totals |
| `pods_loaded(S)` | int | 0 or 1 | Web Map & Transit (registry `pod_body`) | Pod bodies in the world (0 before `init_world`, 1 after; the 453 figure includes it) |
| `TILES_WORLD_MVP` | int tiles | 600 | LY D.13 (a Layout knob citing FT:598; not a registry entry) | World tile budget |
| `WORLD_TILES_WARN_PCT` | int % | 90 | LY D.13 | Warning fraction (WARN at 540 tiles) |

**Output Range:** 0 to `TILES_WORLD_MVP` + 4; the MVP content spans 182 (`{home}`) to 453 (all three ships plus a docked pod). Per-section bounds are Layout's (`SECTION_MAX_TILES_W` 48 by `SECTION_MAX_TILES_H` 32, `MAX_ROOMS_PER_SECTION` 8); the authored `size_tiles` are 16 x 18, 17 x 14 and 20 x 18.
**Example:** `home_ship` 42 + 39 + 36 + 35 + 30 = 182; `derelict_a` 20 + 33 + 30 + 35 = 118; `derelict_b` 20 + 39 + 40 + 20 + 30 = 149. S0 `{home}` 182 (30.3 %); S1 `{home, A}` 300 (50.0 %); S2 and S3 `{home, B}` 331 (55.2 %); S4 `{home+B, A}` 449 (74.8 %); S4 with a docked pod 453 (75.5 %). Every set sits 87 tiles or more under the 540 WARN line, so `TILE_BUDGET` is silent at MVP and tracks the budget for the Vertical Slice.

### D.2 Count tolerance (one-sided)

The `count_tolerance` formula is defined as:

`over = authored - target` ; `tol = max(floor(target x NODE_DRIFT_PCT / 100), NODE_DRIFT_ABS_MIN)` ; `NODE_COUNT_DRIFT = WARN iff over > tol` ; `INFO iff over < 0` ; `ERROR iff sum of authored nodes over the world > NODE_HARD_MAX`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `authored` | int | 0 to 120 | the templates (DA-19 ledger) | Count of nodes, design edges or LOAD nodes for one section |
| `target` | int | the "about" counts of LSG LS-26 | LSG LS-26 (LSG:228) | Nodes about 40 / 30 / 35 (home / A / B); edges about 55 (home) and about 45 (the B cost row of LSG D.16); LOADs about 8 (home). LS-26 gives A no edge or LOAD target |
| `NODE_DRIFT_PCT` | int % | 10 to 30 (20) | Authoring knob | Relative tolerance |
| `NODE_DRIFT_ABS_MIN` | int | 2 to 5 (3) | Authoring knob | Absolute floor so a small target is not a hair-trigger |
| `NODE_HARD_MAX` | int | 120 | LSG:787 (the Graph's MVP content bound; `MAX_GRAPH_NODES` 4096 and the 800-node WARN of LSG:915 are irrelevant at this scale) | Hard cap on authored nodes in the world |

**Output Range:** an INFO, a WARN or a pass per count; below target is never a defect, because the Graph's cost model (LSG D.16) is monotone in count.
**Example:** home nodes 42 against 40: over 2, `tol` max(8, 3) = 8, pass. A nodes 27 against 30: over -3, INFO. B nodes 35 against 35: over 0. World nodes 104 against 120: pass. Home LOADs 11 against 8: over 3, `tol` max(floor(1.6), 3) = 3; the lint warns only when over **exceeds** `tol`, so 3 passes with no margin (one more lamp or motor on `home_ship` fires the WARN). Home design edges 34 against about 55: over -21, INFO (a tree of n nodes needs n - 1 edges, DA-9, so 34 is the structural minimum for this node set, DA-19); B edges 29 against about 45: INFO. Per-kind edge targets in LS-26 ("about 8 CABLEs", "about 5 DUCTs", "3 PIPEs") are reported as INFO and never linted: the home power tree has 16 CABLE edges because 17 power nodes need 16 (DA-20), twice LS-26's "about 8", which is flagged in Edge Cases and Open Questions as a wording tension with the Graph's estimate, not a defect.

### D.3 Fixture bound use (Layout D.16)

The `fixture_bound_use` formula is defined as:

`fixtures_max(V) = min(6, 1 + floor_div(V, 8))` ; `room_ok = (FIXTURES_MIN <= n_fix <= fixtures_max(V))` ; `set_ok(S) = (sum over rooms in S of n_fix <= FIXTURE_WORLD_MAX)` ; `emitter_tally(S) = fixtures(S) + lanterns(S) + beacons(S) + glow_bound(S) <= MAX_EMITTERS`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `V` | int tiles | 4 to 200 | LY D.3 | Room volume |
| `n_fix` | int | 1 to 6 | DA-22 tables | Authored fixtures (lamp housings, lit or dark) in the room |
| `FIXTURES_MIN` | int | 1 | LY D.16 `[PLACEHOLDER: Emitter AC-58]` | A dark housing still counts (EM-13) |
| `FIXTURE_WORLD_MAX` | int | 28 | EM AC-58 `[PLACEHOLDER: Emitter]` | Fixtures per loaded set |
| `lanterns(S)`, `beacons(S)` | int | 0 to 8 / 0 to 1 | DA-24 | Authored NODE_LANTERNs and BEACONs in the set |
| `glow_bound(S)` | int | 0 to 10 | D.12 | Fault-glow rows bound for the set (D.12 now defines it at set level) |
| `MAX_EMITTERS` `[PLACEHOLDER: Emitter]` | int | 96 (registry); the Emitter derives 70 for authored content | registry; EM AC-58 | Emitter cap |

**Output Range:** `fixtures_max` is 1 (V below 8) to 6 (V of 40 or more). The per-set sum is 8 to 21 in MVP content.
**Example:** `cryo_bay` V 42: `min(6, 1 + 5)` = 6, authored 2, pass. `b_pump` V 20: `min(6, 1 + 2)` = 3, authored 1, pass. `airlock` V 30: 4, authored 1. `b_reactor` V 40: 6, authored 2. Maxima by room: home 6, 5, 5, 5, 4; A 3, 5, 4, 5; B 3, 5, 6, 3, 4. Set sums: S0 8, S1 14, S2 and S3 15, S4 21, all at or under 28. Emitter tally for S4: 21 fixtures + 8 lanterns + 1 beacon + at most 9 glow rows = 39 authored, at most the Emitter's derived 70. **Dock status glows (DA-46, user ruling X2).** Read conservatively as fixtures the dock rooms are `airlock` 2 of 4, `a_dock` 2 of 3 and `b_dock` 2 of 3, and the set sums are S0 9, S1 16, S2 and S3 17, S4 24, all at or under 28; read as a separate class the S4 tally is 21 + 3 + 8 + 1 + 9 = 42, at most 70 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.

### D.4 Part cover use and access order (Item DB F-11)

The `part_cover_use` formula is defined as:

`part_cover_pm(R) = floor(1000 x sum_f min(supply(f, R), demand(f, R)) / sum_f demand(f, R))` (capped aggregate; per family `floor(1000 x supply(f, R) / demand(f, R))`, `PART_COVER_UNDEFINED` -1 when demand is 0 and the warning `PART_TYPE_UNSUPPLIED` when demand is above 0 and supply is 0; a demand sum of 0 gives `PART_COVER_UNDEFINED`, reported, never divided, G5) ; `in_band = (PART_COVER_MIN_PM <= part_cover_pm <= PART_COVER_MAX_PM)` ; `access_order(part) = the first sortie set whose rooms contain the part's slot` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G2, creative-director lean: capped aggregate)*

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `supply(f, R)` | int per family | 0 to 4 in total at S4 | DA-25 ledger (loose-part placements + DESTROYED module nodes at build) | S4 total 4: A 2 (cable 1, hull 1), B 2 (pipe 1, duct 1), none on `home_ship` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G2, creative-director lean)* |
| `demand(f, R)` | int per family | 5 at S4 (fixed by the list) | IDB F-11 (part-taking FULL-repairable authored faults) | duct 1 (`d_cryo_exposed`), cable 1 (`cab_trunk`), pipe 2 (`p_cryo_rad`, `pf_feed`), hull 1 (`seg_b_pump_n`); excluded and logged: the O2 seam, the broken junction, the pump stall, the SCRAM *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G2, creative-director lean)* |
| `PART_COVER_MIN_PM`, `PART_COVER_MAX_PM` | int permille | 500 / 800 | IDB F-11 (advisory band; variables of registry formula `part_cover_pm`) | `PART_COVER_BAND` is a WARN |

**Output Range:** 0 to 1000 (the aggregate is capped at each family's own demand; a per-family value can exceed 1000 when that family is over-supplied); the MVP value at S4 is 800, inside the band at the top edge *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G2, creative-director lean)*. `PART_COVER_BAND` checks the capped aggregate at S4 and each family with demand >= 2 (pipe, 500); a family with demand 1 reads only 0 or 1000 or more, so it is printed, not band-checked.
**Example:** at S4, floor(1000 x (1 + 1 + 1 + 1) / 5) = 800, pass. Per legal loaded set on the capped sum: S0 {home}: demand 4 (duct 1, cable 1, pipe 2), supply 0, so 0; S1 {home, A}: demand 4, supply cable 1 + hull 1, capped (1 + 0) / 4, so 250 (hull has no demand until B is loaded; `PART_TYPE_UNSUPPLIED` duct, pipe); S2 and S3 {home, B}: demand 5, supply duct 1 + pipe 1 (the plate is on A), floor(1000 x 2 / 5) = 400 (unsupplied: cable, hull); S4: 800. Only S4 is band-checked; S0 to S3 are the logged 'uncovered by choice' entries. Knob sweep at S4: 3 parts (no duct) gives 600, in band; 2 parts (no duct, no cable) gives 400, below; a second pipe fitting (5 parts) gives 1000, above; a second hull plate still gives 800 (capped at its own demand). Family cover: duct 1 of 1, cable 1 of 1, hull 1 of 1, pipe 1 of 2; uncovered by choice: the O2 seam and the broken junction (the spare breaker covers the junction in kind). Access order: `part_hull_plate` (A, `a_spine`) and `part_cable_splice` (A, `a_machine`, inside the lethal cold room) are reached on the first sortie; the `part_pipe_fitting` (B, `b_pump` floor) and `part_duct_coupling` (B, across the vacuum) on the second. The first fault of the game (the cryo-bay coolant pipe) therefore cannot be fully repaired until the second sortie, about 45 to 55 minutes in (a pacing estimate: sortie A 15 to 20 minutes, sortie B 20 to 25, travel), which at the D.15 patch cadence is about 20 patch actions on the home leaks; that scarcity is deliberate (P5) and G-0 holds throughout.

### D.5 Escape-route use, run, walk and carry (Layout D.19)

The `escape_route_use` formula is defined as:

`budget_px(v) = W_MIN_WARNING_S x TICK_RATE x v` ; `ESCAPE_ROUTE_OVER_BUDGET iff min over closable doors d of route_length_px(anchor, d) > budget_px(V_RUN)` ; `walk_ticks = ceil(route_length_px / v)` (the `n_closed = 0` term of Boarding & Salvage's `route_ticks`) ; `escape_slack_ticks = W_MIN_WARNING_S x TICK_RATE - walk_ticks - door_close_ticks`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `W_MIN_WARNING_S` | int s | 10 | registry | The fairness window (endogenous transitions only, LSG LS-13) |
| `TICK_RATE` | int | 60 | registry | Ticks per second |
| `v` | int px per tick | `V_RUN` 3, `V_WALK` 2, `V_CARRY` 1 | registry | Run, walk, carry speeds |
| `route_length_px` | int px | 0 to the section's span | Layout read (LY D.19; `ESCAPE_ROUTE_BUDGET_PX` is Layout's, not a registry entry) | Path length from a fault anchor to a closable door |
| `door_close_ticks` | int | about 60 `[UNV: Interaction]` | Interaction door channel | Taken as 60 ticks (about 1 s) by the lint, reported as unverified (it was taken as 0, which hid that the carry slack is only 120 ticks before the door) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `walk_ticks` | int ticks | 0 to 600 at the budget | derived | Travel time from anchor to door, `ceil(route_length_px / v)`; the `n_closed = 0` term of Boarding & Salvage's registry formula `route_ticks`, which this GDD does not redefine |

**Output Range:** `budget_px` is 1,800 at `V_RUN`, 1,200 at `V_WALK`, 600 at `V_CARRY`; `escape_slack_ticks` runs up to 540 (9 s) with `door_close_ticks` 60 and is zero at the budget less the door.
**Example:** `pf_feed` to `door_engine_n`, about 480 px (Layout's read governs): `walk_ticks` running 160 (2.7 s), slack 380 ticks (6.3 s); walking 240 ticks, slack 300; carrying 480 ticks, slack 60 ticks (1.0 s) with `door_close_ticks` 60 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. `p_cryo_rad` to `door_cryo_s`, about 336 px: running 112 ticks (1.9 s). `seg_b_pump_n` to `door_b_spine_pump`, about 384 px: running 128 ticks (2.1 s). The longest, 480 px, is 27 % of the 1,800 budget. A route over 600 px is legal on foot and fails only `ESCAPE_ROUTE_CARRY` (ERROR when the carry slack is below 0, WARN under 120 ticks) when a salvageable module node (a module in hand moves at `V_CARRY` 1, PC-8) shares the room with a live endogenous fault; a stowed HEAVY part, the 30 kg plate included, does not change `V_CARRY`, but while it is stowed run is disabled (user ruling D18, 2026-10-27), so a plate-bearing player moves at `V_WALK` 2 and the lint also prints the escape budget at `V_WALK` (1,200 px): `pf_feed` 480 px is 240 ticks with slack 300, `p_cryo_rad` 336 px is 168 ticks with slack 372, `seg_b_pump_n` 384 px is 192 ticks with slack 348 (each = 600 - walk ticks - `door_close_ticks` 60), the longest being 40 % of 1,200 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.

### D.6 Residual-light clock, recharge and withdrawal

The `residual_light_clock` formula is defined as:

`t_lit_steps = floor(charge_dJ / req_w)` ; `t_lit_withdrawn = floor((charge_dJ - withdrawn_dJ) / req_w)` ; `t_recharge_steps = NO_SOURCE if S = 0 or min(S - sum_served, CH_CAP) <= 0, else ceil((BATT_CELL - stock) / min(S - sum_served, CH_CAP))` ; `exterior_lit = (lamp node state in {OK, DEGRADED} and persisted island stock > 0)` for a built wreck, `(charge_dJ > 0)` for a never-built one

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `charge_dJ` | int dJ | 180,000 to 1,440,000 | authored: `b_a1` 540,000, `b_b1` 432,000 (DA-27); BATTERY `stock` | Island battery stock at build; never a percent (DA-4) |
| `req_w` | int W = dJ per step | 20 (fixed) | EM-11; `mod_lamp` | The lamp is the island's only load |
| `BATT_CELL` | int dJ | 3,600,000 | LSG D.6 variable table; IDB:220 | A full cell; only the derivation base |
| `withdrawn_dJ` | int dJ | 0 to `charge_dJ` | Suit's top-up (SE) | Stock removed by a `withdraw_store`; a shipping input at MVP (user ruling X5, *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*) |
| `S`, `sum_served`, `CH_CAP` | int W | S 0 to 10000; `CH_CAP` 0 to 500 (300) | LSG D.6 | Source output, served power, recharge cap (after the tie is closed on a powered bus) |

**Output Range:** `t_lit_steps` 9,000 to 72,000 for stocks of 180,000 to 1,440,000 dJ; the clock counts **loaded** time only, because an unloaded section's slots are skipped (LSG LS-17).
**Example:** A: 540,000 / 20 = 27,000 steps = 2,700 s = **45.0 minutes** of loaded time; B: 432,000 / 20 = 21,600 steps = **36.0 minutes**. A Suit top-up that withdraws 100,000 dJ from `b_a1` leaves floor(440,000 / 20) = 22,000 steps (36.7 minutes). Recharge on B after V12 and a closed `j_b_res` with surplus of at least 300 W: ceil(3,168,000 / 300) = 10,560 steps = 17.6 minutes to a full cell. On A there is no reactor (`S` 0), so the guard returns `NO_SOURCE` and A's residual battery never recharges: closing `j_a_res` merges `b_a1` into the bus pool (`C_bus`, D.16) and the lamp then drains with the bus *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The last step is a one-step dim and then dark, never a fade for effect (the lamp is steady, unmodulated, DA-27). The exterior rule is the Web Map & Transit contract `[provisional: Web Map & Transit undesigned]`.

### D.7 Traverse bound use (Suit D.11)

The `traverse_bound_use` formula is defined as:

*(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: conformed to Suit's registered vacuum budget, G1/G6; the one-way `RESERVE_S / 2` bound is withdrawn by Suit D.11 and by this GDD)* The lint reads Suit's exported budget (registry `VACUUM_BUDGET_S`, source `suit-and-equipment.md`; Suit D.11, AC-SE-75a, build assertion A15) and does not restate it as a free-standing constant:

`leg_runs = ceil((HOP_LEG_TICKS + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS + IN_POD_SLACK_TICKS) / GRAPH_STEP_TICKS)` (163 at the defaults) ; `traverse_runs = sum over the path's out and back legs of ceil(tiles_leg x TILE_PX / (V_leg x GRAPH_STEP_TICKS))`, with `V_leg` = `V_WALK` 2 on a leg walked empty and `V_CARRY` 1 on a leg a module is carried ; `TRAVERSE_TOO_LONG iff traverse_runs + 2 x leg_runs > (RESERVE_S - RESERVE_WARN_S) x RUNS_PER_S` (600 at the defaults), written in the cross-multiplied integer form `traverse_runs > VACUUM_BUDGET_RUNS`, where `VACUUM_BUDGET_RUNS = (RESERVE_S - RESERVE_WARN_S) x RUNS_PER_S - 2 x leg_runs` (274; `VACUUM_BUDGET_S` 27 is its seconds form, read from the registry)

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tiles_leg` | int | 0 to 68 | the authored path across the unbreathable compartment(s), per leg | Path length of one leg in tiles (a Manhattan bound, width plus depth, for a room; summed over nested unbreathable rooms on that leg) |
| `V_leg` | int px per tick | `V_WALK` 2 or `V_CARRY` 1 | registry | Speed on that leg: walking empty out, carrying a module back (the lint's reading); a both-ways-carried reading is reported only |
| `TILE_PX` | int px | 48 | registry | Tile size |
| `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`, `RUNS_PER_S` | int | 6, 10 | registry; Suit D.8 | Ticks per Graph run; runs per second (`TICK_RATE` / `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`) |
| `RESERVE_S`, `RESERVE_WARN_S` | int s | 90, 30 | registry (Suit-owned, `[PLACEHOLDER: Suit]`); SE D.11 | The suit's vacuum or cold reserve and the warn edge the breath clause never reaches |
| `HOP_LEG_TICKS`, `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS`, `IN_POD_SLACK_TICKS` | int ticks | 900, 45, 30 | registry (`HOP_LEG_TICKS`, `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS`); `IN_POD_SLACK_TICKS` is Web Map D.2's derived 30 | The pod leg the reserve also pays for, twice (out and back) |
| `VACUUM_BUDGET_RUNS` | int runs | 274 | derived (Suit D.11); `VACUUM_BUDGET_S` 27 registry | The breath clause's remaining budget for the sortie's unbreathable walking |

**Output Range:** `VACUUM_BUDGET_RUNS` 274 runs (27 s) after two default legs (`2 x 163` = 326 of 600). In tiles: **22 tiles each way when walking out and carrying back** (22 x (4 + 8) = 264, under 274; 23 gives 276 and fails), **34 tiles in total when carried both ways** (34 x 8 = 272; 35 gives 280 and fails), **68 tiles in total walking** (68 x 4 = 272). The former figures (112 walking and 56 carrying, one-way from `RESERVE_S / 2`, and the 56-tile round trip this GDD derived from it) are withdrawn. The lint prints the legs, `traverse_runs`, the budget and the slack in runs.
**Example:** `b_breach` (6 x 5): about 11 tiles each way = 11 x 12 = 132 runs, pass with 142 runs of slack. B's nested worst case (`b_pump` also decompressed, 9 more tiles each way) is 20 tiles each way = 20 x 12 = 240 runs, **34 runs (204 ticks) under 274: PASS only when read by legs** (walk out, carry back). Read as carried both ways (40 tiles = 320 runs) it would fail, so the lint reads legs, and B's nested 40-tile case has only 34 runs of slack; the carry-both-ways reading used by Boarding & Salvage D.4 is a conservative report-only figure and not the gate. A hypothetical 30-tile one-way corridor would fail (30 x 12 = 360 over 274). The warmth clause of Suit D.11 governs a deep-cold room and is checked by D.10 (`COLD_WORK_BUDGET`), not here.

### D.8 Containment colour count (Lighting LO-33, PD-1)

The `containment_colour_count` formula is defined as:

`edge(r1, r2) iff gap_px(r1, r2) <= R_DRAW_MAX_PX + WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` ; `colours_needed = colours used by greedy colouring of the proximity graph in room-table order` ; `CONTAIN_COLOUR_REPORT = ERROR iff colours_needed > CONTAIN_COLOURS`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `gap_px` | int px | 0 upward | Layout's room rects | Euclidean distance between the closest points of two room rectangles |
| `R_DRAW_MAX_PX` | int px | 126 | Lighting (LO; the FIXTURE `r_draw` at 1000 permille, drawn to `r_off`) | Longest light draw reach *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` | int px | 24 | registry | Wall strip depth; the edge radius is 150 px (no MVP room pair has a gap in (146, 150], so the colouring is unchanged at 3; the lint's output governs) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `CONTAIN_COLOURS` | int | 4 `[PLACEHOLDER: Lighting]` | registry | Available containment colours |

**Output Range:** 1 to `CONTAIN_COLOURS` for a legal body; a larger value is a CONFLICT paragraph to Lighting, never a re-shaped room (DA-31). The proximity edge is geometric, so the count is the same with every door open and every door closed; the lint runs and prints both.
**Example:** home edges: cryo-corridor, cryo-cargo, corridor-cargo, corridor-engine, corridor-airlock, engine-airlock (all 24 px gaps); cargo-airlock and cryo-engine are 192 px, no edge. Greedy colours: cryo 1, corridor 2, cargo 3, engine 1, airlock 3 = **3**. Derelict A: the clique `a_spine`, `a_life`, `a_machine` = **3**. Derelict B alone: the cliques `b_spine`, `b_reactor`, `b_pump` and `b_reactor`, `b_pump`, `b_breach` (the 72 px exterior notch leaves a 120 px gap, inside 150) = **3**. Home + B after the weld at offset (696, 384): `b_dock` is 48 px from `airlock` and a diagonal about 76 px from the corridor (corner (672, 552) to (744, 576): dx 72, dy 24, still inside 150), so it takes colour 1, and the body still needs **3**. 3 of `CONTAIN_COLOURS` 4: margin 1. Two of the 14 rooms (`spine_corridor`, `b_pump`) are PD-1 exceptions under Lighting's rewritten per-face C3 (DA-31).

### D.9 PWR-OVL window, trigger and trunk cap

The `pwr_ovl_window` formula is defined as:

`rho_pm = floor(1000 x L_below / cap_W)` ; `dhp_ovl = min(DHP_MAX, floor(OVL_K x max(0, rho_pm - 1000)^2 / 10^4))` per step ; `cap_lo(L) = floor(1000 x L / (OVL_WINDOW_HI_PM + 1)) + 1` ; `cap_hi(L) = floor(1000 x L / OVL_WINDOW_LO_PM)` ; `legal_cap = [max over beacon states of cap_lo, min over beacon states of cap_hi]` ; `OVERLOAD_WINDOW iff OVL_WINDOW_LO_PM <= rho_pm(trigger, beacon off) and rho_pm(trigger, beacon on) <= OVL_WINDOW_HI_PM` ; `EMPTY_WINDOW iff max over beacon states of cap_lo > min over beacon states of cap_hi` (no legal `cap` exists) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `L_below` | int W | 0 to `S` + `D` | the published power tree (DA-20); LSG D.6 derived line (served power in the subtree below the cable) | Served load under `cab_trunk`, the root edge above every LOAD |
| `cap_W` | int W | 500 to 6000 | authored `cap` of `cab_trunk` | **2161 W** |
| `OVL_K`, `DHP_MAX` | int | 4 / 2000 | LSG D.9 | Overload scale and clamp |
| `OVL_WINDOW_LO_PM`, `OVL_WINDOW_HI_PM` | int permille | 1100 / 1111 | derived here from LSG D.15's PWR-OVL band | The `rho_pm` interval in which `dhp_ovl` is exactly 4: `(rho_pm - 1000)^2` in [10000, 12499]; 3 gives 3,334 steps and 5 gives 2,000, both outside 2,500 +-15 % |
| trigger `L` | int W | 2380 (beacon off), 2400 (beacon on) | DA-20 | Standing 1880 W (1900 with the beacon lamp lit) plus one installed `mod_heater` (500 W) |
| `S` | int W | 2500 `[PLACEHOLDER: Item DB out_w_rated]` | LSG D.6 | Reactor supply that clamps served power |

**Output Range:** `dhp_ovl` is 0 at `rho_pm` of 1000 or below, 4 inside the window, up to `DHP_MAX` 2000. The legal `cap` for a 500 W trigger over the 1880 W standing load is cap_lo(2380) = 2141 to cap_hi(2380) = 2163 with the beacon off and cap_lo(2400) = 2159 to cap_hi(2400) = 2181 with it on: the intersection is **2159 to 2163 W**, and 2161 sits in the middle. `OVERLOAD_WINDOW` derives this interval from the published tree at lint time and fails an authored `cap` outside it (2161 is the authored mid-point, not a lint constant); `EMPTY_WINDOW` (ERROR) fires when the interval is empty. The width is about `L_trigger` / 102 less 18 W (the beacon swing of 20 W costs the window almost all of its width): 5 W for the 500 W trigger, 2 W (1889 to 1890 W, with 1871 to 1890 W the beacon-off half alone) for a 200 W heater, and empty below an `L_trigger` of about 1,840 W *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Both limits are solved, not tuned (a 2150 W cap fails the beacon-on state, rho 1116 and `dhp_ovl` 5; a 2000 W cap gives rho 1190 and `dhp_ovl` 14, DESTROYED in 71 s).
**Example:** at `cap` 2161 W the standing load 1880 W gives `rho_pm` 869 (879 with the beacon lit, 573 at wake with `j_cryo` open), no damage. The trigger, an installed `htr_a`, gives L 2380, `rho_pm` 1101, `dhp_ovl` floor(4 x 101^2 / 10^4) = 4; with the beacon on, L 2400, `rho_pm` 1110, `dhp_ovl` floor(4 x 110^2 / 10^4) = 4. Timeline at 4 bp per step from 10000: `hp` below `HP_OK` 7000 after 751 steps (75 s, DEGRADED, the tell starts), at or below `HP_FAIL` 3000 after 1,750 steps (2.9 minutes, FAILED), 0 after 2,500 steps (4.2 minutes, DESTROYED), reproducing LSG D.15's PWR-OVL row (4.5 minutes, 3.8 to 5.2). The fairness gate never engages: 1,750 - 751 = 999 steps against `W_MIN_STEPS` 100. Other adds at `cap` 2161: a lamp (1900 W, rho 879) and a door motor (2000 W, rho 925) are safe; a `mod_coolant_pump` (2280 W, rho 1055, 1 bp per step) is DESTROYED in 10,000 steps (16.7 minutes); a `mod_scrubber` (2480 W, rho 1147, 8 bp per step) in 1,250 steps (2.1 minutes), a harsher player mistake that was outside the authored fixture by design and is now reachable through the DA-47 scrubber slots (user ruling D72; the install states are tabulated there) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; any larger add is supply-clamped to S = 2500 W, rho 1156, 9 bp per step, 1,112 steps (1.9 minutes) at least (battery discharge `D` can add more, `[UNV: Graph discharge cap]`). Inrush on the first V3: the cryo branch (640 W) requests floor(640 x 1500 / 1000) = 960 W for `INRUSH_STEPS` 5, trunk L 2200, rho 1018, `dhp_ovl` 0. A blackout recovery that re-energises everything at once is supply-clamped to rho 1156 for 5 steps, 9 x 5 = 45 bp of scorch per recovery. **Welded body:** closing the seam breaker on a SCRAMmed `r_b` loads the trunk to 1880 + 1240 = 3120 W, supply-clamped to S 2500 W, rho 1156, 9 bp per step, DESTROYED (`NOT_REPAIRABLE`) in 1,112 steps (about 1.9 minutes), the same line as the clamped adds above; the rule, the tell and the recovery window (re-open the breaker before step 778 and the trunk survives; DESTROYED only from step 1,112) are in DA-20.

**Lamps-off lever (design review 2026-10-27).** Switching lamps off is the player's first lever on PWR-OVL and it moves the fault inside and out of its authored band. With the trigger installed and `j_cryo` closed (L 2380 W, beacon off, `cap` 2161 W), k lamps switched off at 20 W each, k from 0 to 7:

| Lamps off k | L (W) | `rho_pm` | `dhp_ovl` (bp per step) | Steps to DESTROYED |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 2380 | 1101 | 4 | 2,500 |
| 1 | 2360 | 1092 | 3 | 3,333 |
| 2 | 2340 | 1082 | 2 | 5,000 |
| 3 | 2320 | 1073 | 2 | 5,000 |
| 4 | 2300 | 1064 | 1 | 10,000 |
| 5 | 2280 | 1055 | 1 | 10,000 |
| 6 | 2260 | 1045 | 0 | none (OVERLOAD sev 1 only) |
| 7 | 2240 | 1036 | 0 | none |

With the beacon lit every row moves one lamp (add 20 W). The authored band (LSG D.15, 3.8 to 5.2 minutes) is the build state with every lamp on, which is what `OVERLOAD_WINDOW` and AC-DA-28 hold; switching lamps off is a legitimate response that slows the fault (k 1 to 5) or defuses its damage while the hum keeps sounding (k 6 and 7), so it is a lever and not a defect. The lint prints this table (AC-DA-141) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

### D.10 Cold-room exposure budget (the lethal cold room)

The `cold_room_exposure` formula is defined as (Suit D.4 used verbatim, not restated; the Authoring-side use is the margin check):

`e = onset_cold - T` ; `gain = TL_GAIN_MIN + (TL_GAIN_MAX - TL_GAIN_MIN) x min(e, TL_DEPTH_CAP_MC) / TL_DEPTH_CAP_MC` (integer) ; `runs_critical = ceil(TL_CRITICAL / gain)` ; `runs_failed = ceil(TL_MAX / gain)` ; `cross_ticks = depth_tiles x TILE_PX / V_CARRY` ; `equilibrium = T_AMB_MC + ceil(100000 / (K_HULL x S_ext))` ; `t_work_max = runs_critical / 10 - (in_leg_s + out_leg_s)` seconds *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `onset_cold` | int mC | -20000 | `COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC` -15000 less `INSUL_MC` 5000 `[PLACEHOLDER: Suit]` (SE D.4; LSG:1040) | Shifted cold-damage onset, at full suit integrity (at or above `SUIT_LEAK_HP`); a leaking suit moves the onset toward `COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC` -15000 (SE-21: `insul_eff` = `INSUL_MC` x min(`integrity_bp`, `SUIT_LEAK_HP`) / `SUIT_LEAK_HP`), and `COLD_ROOM_DRIFT` also prints the -15000 projection |
| `T` | int mC | -30000 to -20000 | authored `T0` (`a_machine` -22000) | Room temperature |
| `TL_GAIN_MIN`, `TL_GAIN_MAX`, `TL_DEPTH_CAP_MC`, `TL_CRITICAL`, `TL_MAX` | int | 8 / 40 / 20000 / 4000 / 12000 | Suit knobs (SE D.4) | Exposure gain per run and the two thresholds |
| `S_ext` | int tiles | 13 for `a_machine` | DA-7 `exposed_tiles` | Hull tiles exposed |
| `K_HULL` | int | 25 | LSG D.7 | Hull conductance |
