| `depth_tiles` | int | 7 for `a_machine` | C.2 | Door to far wall |

**Output Range:** 10 Graph runs per second. At `T0` the room is CRITICAL after 364 runs (36.4 s) and FAILED after 1,091 (109.1 s); as the room cools toward its equilibrium the gain rises and the margins shrink.
**Example:** `a_machine` `T0` -22000: e 2000, gain 8 + 32 x 2000 / 20000 = 11, `runs_critical` ceil(4000 / 11) = 364, `runs_failed` ceil(12000 / 11) = 1,091. Equilibrium: -30000 + ceil(100000 / 325) = -30000 + 308 = -29692 mC, far below -20000 (SE-21 satisfied, `[derived; Graph owner to confirm with LSG D.7, OQ-DA-4]`); there e = 9692, gain 8 + 32 x 9692 / 20000 = 23, `runs_critical` 174 (17.4 s). Crossing: 7 x 48 / 1 = 336 ticks = 5.6 s carrying, 3.1 times shorter than the worst-case CRITICAL time (reported and reviewed, not a lint threshold). Crossing is safe and lingering is lethal: the patch channel inside is the dangerous act (DA-28).

**In-room work budget (design review 2026-10-27).** `t_work_max` is the time left for channels inside `a_machine` before CRITICAL, with the conservative 7-tile legs at `V_CARRY` (5.6 s each way, a module in hand both ways): at `T0` 36.4 - 11.2 = **25.2 s**; after 30 minutes unheated (T -25,417 mC, gain 16, `runs_critical` 250) 25.0 - 11.2 = **13.8 s**; at the equilibrium (gain 23, 174 runs) 17.4 - 11.2 = **6.2 s**; with the heater on and the room above the shifted onset the budget is unbounded for the window of D.22. The longest single in-room act is the `htr_a` cut, `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` 240 `[PLACEHOLDER: Interaction]` = 4.0 s, and a PATCH is 3.0 s: both fit the 6.2 s floor. Two channels in one visit (cut `j_a_main`, install the spare, 8.0 s) do not, so they belong inside the D.22 window or in two visits. Boarding & Salvage D.4 computes the same dwell as `margin_pm` of `T_crit` (546 ticks, 250 permille at `T0` for the `htr_a` cut); the lint `COLD_WORK_BUDGET` enforces it and AC-DA-138 measures it.

### D.11 Dead-fault back-date

The `dead_fault_back_date` formula is defined as:

`fail_since_step = -(DRIED_AFTER_STEPS + DRY_MARGIN_STEPS)` ; `since_steps(step_no) = step_no - fail_since_step` ; `DEAD_FAULT_NOT_DRIED iff -fail_since_step < DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` for every authored subject at `hp` <= `HP_FAIL` on an unserved branch

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` | int steps | 12000 `[PLACEHOLDER: Failure & Trail]` (registry range 7500 to 36000) | registry; FT:788 | Steps after which a trail's final stop is drawn |
| `DRY_MARGIN_STEPS` | int steps | 600 to 26000 (2400) | Authoring knob | Margin so a modest F&T retune does not re-wet the fault |
| `fail_since_step` | signed int steps | -14400 at the placeholder | Graph node field (LS-3, FT:317); the sign is accepted by M1 (LSG:182, OQ-DA-1 answered 2026-10-27) | The step the subject notionally failed at |

**Output Range:** `since_steps` is 14,400 at step 0, above the 12,000 threshold by 2,400. If F&T raises `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` above 14,400 the lint fails every dead fault, which is the intended tripwire; back-dating to -(36000 + 2400) = -38400 would cover the whole registered range but is outside it as a trail input (EC below).
A LIVE fault may also be back-dated (CLT-LEAK, -60) so that its trail age at wake matches the fixture; it is not on the `DEAD_FAULT_NOT_DRIED` scope list; M1's acceptance (LSG:182, OQ-DA-1) covers it too.
**Example:** `pa_frozen`, `j_a_main`, `pb_leak` and `seg_b_breach` are each authored at `fail_since_step` -14400: at step 0 `since_steps` = 14,400, at least 12,000, pass. At 18,000 the lint reports four ERRORs naming the four records, the owner (Failure & Trail) and both numbers; no auto-fix.

### D.12 Fault-glow cascade bound

The `fault_glow_cascade_bound` formula is defined as:

`glow_bound(F_set) = ceil(1.25 x F_set) = floor((125 x F_set + 99) / 100)` ; `FAULT_GLOW_BOUND iff glow_bound(F_set) > MAX_FAULT_GLOW` ; `F_set` is the most glow rows active at once over the whole loaded set, the bodies summed before the ceiling and not after *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `F_set` | int | 0 to 8 | the declared cascade hops (DA-15) | Most faults with a glow row active at once over the loaded set, counting woken dead faults and declared cascade hops; per-body figures are added before the ceiling |
| `MAX_FAULT_GLOW` | int | 10 `[PLACEHOLDER: Emitter]` | registry | Cap on simultaneous fault-glow emitters |
| 1.25 | fixed ratio | 125 / 100 | EM D.15 I-CAPS (c) | Cascade allowance |

**Output Range:** 0 at `F_set` 0 to 10 at `F_set` 8 (the cap is reached exactly at 8); `F_set` 9 gives 12 and fails.
**Example:** welded body (home + B): O2-SHIP, PWR-OVL, FUEL-DRIP, O2-ROOM and B's SCRAM rim row = 5 gives (625 + 99) / 100 = 7; after V12 wakes B's dead coolant fault the worst case is 6, giving 8. Both at or under 10 (margin 2). Derelict A alone: the broken-breaker arc (1 glow row; STARVE and FREEZE have none) gives 2 (ceil(1.25) = 2). The welded-body worst case (6 rows, bound 8) counts B's woken coolant fault conservatively: a coolant LEAK has no glow row (EM-17), so that figure is an overcount that breaches no cap. The list in DA-15 is the "declared cascade hops" the Emitter asks for. **Set level:** S4 `{home+B, A}` carries at most 6 (welded body) + 1 (A's arc) = 7 rows, so `glow_bound` is ceil(8.75) = **9**, margin 1; summing the per-body bounds (8 + 2) put S4 at 10, exactly on the cap with no margin *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

### D.13 Weld offset (Layout D.6)

The `weld_offset` formula is defined as:

`offset_b = offset_a + pos_a - pos_b` ; `pos = (line_px, start_px)` for an E or W facing ; `LATTICE_OK iff offset_b.x mod 24 = 0 and offset_b.y mod 24 = 0` ; `GEOM_OVERLAP iff any rect of B's rects_in (rooms, wall runs, jambs, props, hatches) overlaps any rect of the home body after the offset` (Layout LY-14, LY D.6; cited, not restated)

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `offset_a` | int px pair | (0, 0) | the home body's section offset | The host section is the origin |
| `pos_a` | int px pair | (720, 672) | `sock_airlock_e` (DA-10) | Host socket position |
| `pos_b` | int px pair | (24, 288) | `sock_b_w` (DA-10) | Mate socket position |

**Output Range:** a pair of multiples of 24 (the 24 px lattice, LY-3); non-multiples are a Layout ERROR.
**Example:** (0, 0) + (720, 672) - (24, 288) = **(696, 384)**, both multiples of 24 (29 x 24 and 16 x 24). B's west hull wall x [24, 48) lands at [720, 744), abutting the home east wall [696, 720); B's hatch span y [288, 384) lands at [672, 768), identical to the home socket span. `b_dock` occupies x [744, 936), y [576, 816), clear of the home `airlock` (x below 696) and of `spine_corridor` (x below 672, y below 552). Wall strips: `spine_corridor` is 13 tiles wide (x 48 to 672), so the home corridor's east hull wall is x [672, 696); it and B's west dock wall x [720, 744) are separated by the airlock's east wall [696, 720), so no rect pair overlaps: no `GEOM_OVERLAP`. The hulls abut along x 720 for the airlock's east wall (y [576, 816), containing the socket span [672, 768)); Layout allows contact outside the socket and counts it with its `HULL_CONTACT_OUTSIDE_SOCKET` diagnostic (LY Edge Cases). (The corridor was shortened from 14 tiles to 13 so that no wall strip of the two bodies shares a cell; D.1, D.3 and D.8 carry the re-run figures.)

### D.14 Leaky-kind wear clock

The `leaky_kind_wear_clock` formula is defined as:

`dp = floor(W_base x rate_pm x stress_pm / 10^6)` bp per wear period ; `periods_to(hp0, thr) = ceil((hp0 - thr) / dp)` ; `T_s = periods x WEAR_PERIOD_STEPS / 10` seconds of **loaded** section time ; `T_destroy = periods_to(hp0, 0)`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `W_base` | int bp per 100 steps | 16 (`LEAK_WEAR_BP_PER_100`), 50 (`HULL_FATIGUE_BP_PER_100`) | LSG D.9 | The wear terms that apply to the subject (here the leak term) |
| `rate_pm` | int permille | 1000 HOME; 2000 DERELICT | LSG D.9 derived line = 10^6 / `DERELICT_FAULT_COMPRESSION` 500 | Section time multiplier |
| `stress_pm` | int permille | 1000 (2000 under `BREACH_PROPAGATION_MULT`) | LSG D.9 | Not stressed in authored content |
| `WEAR_PERIOD_STEPS` | int steps | 100 | LSG D.9 | Wear lumps every 10 s |
| `hp0`, `thr` | int bp | 0 to 3999 for a LEAK-mode subject; `HP_FAIL` 3000 or 0 | DA-17 and DA-15 tables | Authored `hp`; threshold FAILED or DESTROYED |

**Output Range:** `dp` is 16 HOME and 32 DERELICT for a leak. For a LEAK-mode subject at `hp0` 3999, 250 periods (41.7 minutes) to DESTROYED on HOME and 125 periods (20.8 minutes) on a DERELICT.
**Example (gated LSG D.9, the binding reading since 2026-10-27; the figures are flowing time, and the literal column is kept as the worst-case contingency):**

| Subject | Section | `hp0` | `dp` | To FAILED | To DESTROYED (`T_destroy`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `p_cryo_rad` (CLT-LEAK, live) | HOME | 3800 | 16 | 50 periods = 8.3 min | 238 periods = 39.7 min |
| `d_cryo_exposed` (O2-SHIP, behind the closed `v_o2_cryo`) | HOME | 2000 | 16 | already FAILED | held at 2000 while the valve is shut; **125 periods = 20.8 min counted from V5, reachable only with an unbounded pool** (the real pool empties first and the leak stops with it, D.19) |
| `pf_feed` (FUEL-DRIP, live) | HOME | 3999 | 16 | 63 periods = 10.5 min | 250 periods = 41.7 min |
| `pa_frozen` (dead, forced FREEZE) | DERELICT | 2500 | 32 | already FAILED | not applicable while forced FREEZE (not in mode LEAK) `[UNV: Graph]`; if thawed with the pump served, 79 periods = 13.2 min of flowing time |
| `pb_leak` (dead) | DERELICT | 2900 | 32 | already FAILED | held at 2900 while dry; **91 periods = 15.2 min** of flowing time counted from V12 |

**Status (design review, 2026-10-27).** Branch (b), gated, is the Graph's text (LS-11 and LSG D.9 as amended 2026-10-27, LSG:127 and LSG:515; LSG:762: a re-opened valve resumes from the stored `hp`) and it binds: a closed valve holds `hp`, a dry dead leaky fault is stable at any idle time, and the `d_cryo_exposed` clock starts when V5 opens the valve (ASK-G1 and OQ-DA-21 are answered). The literal branch (a) and the valve-OPEN branch (c) are retired as shipping branches and survive only as the contingency of DA-16 if the cited Graph text reverts. *Gate caveat `[UNV: Graph]`:* LSG D.9 floors a FAILED subject at `hp` 1 while the D.11 fairness gate is closed, and that gate opens only after a `warn_since_step` above 0 with `sev` at least 1 for 100 steps; whether an authored-at-build subject carries a set `warn_since_step` is not stated, so the DESTROYED figures above are the **worst case** once flow starts (the node is gone and `NOT_REPAIRABLE`), and the milder case (held at `hp` 1) is what `DEAD_FAULT_STILL_REPAIRABLE` measures (DA-42) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

### D.15 Patch and fatigue treadmills

The `patch_fatigue_treadmill` formula is defined as:

`d_decay = floor(PATCH_DECAY_BP_PER_100 x rate_pm / 1000)` ; `n_dec = ceil((PATCH_CAP_BP - (LEAK_HP - 1)) / d_decay)` ; `T_cycle_steps = PATCH_DECAY_STEPS + n_dec x WEAR_PERIOD_STEPS` ; `d_fat = floor(HULL_FATIGUE_BP_PER_100 x rate_pm / 1000)` ; `n_hold = PATCH_DECAY_STEPS / WEAR_PERIOD_STEPS` (24 periods) ; `n_fat = ceil((PATCH_CAP_BP - (BREACH_HP - 1)) / d_fat)` if that value is at most `n_hold`, else `n_hold + ceil((PATCH_CAP_BP - n_hold x d_fat - (BREACH_HP - 1)) / (d_fat + d_decay))`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PATCH_CAP_BP` | int bp | 6000 (range 4500 to 6900) | registry | `hp` ceiling of a PATCH (M7) |
| `LEAK_HP`, `BREACH_HP` | int bp | 4000, 4000 | registry (Graph-owned); LSG:102, LS-11 | Re-leak and re-breach thresholds |
| `PATCH_DECAY_BP_PER_100`, `PATCH_DECAY_STEPS` | int | 100 / 2400 | LSG D.9 | Patch hold (4 minutes) then decay per period |
| `HULL_FATIGUE_BP_PER_100` | int | 50 | LSG D.9 | Fatigue wear |
| `rate_pm` | int permille | 1000 / 2000 | D.14 | HOME or DERELICT |

**Output Range:** `T_cycle_steps` 3,500 to 4,500 (5.8 to 7.5 minutes); `n_fat` 21 to 30 periods (3.5 minutes on a DERELICT; 5.0 minutes on HOME, where the decay term joins after the 24-period hold).
**Example:** a leaky HOME fault: `d_decay` 100, `n_dec` ceil(2001 / 100) = 21, `T_cycle` 2400 + 2100 = 4,500 steps = **7.5 minutes**, 8.0 patches per hour; on a DERELICT `d_decay` 200, `n_dec` 11, `T_cycle` 3,500 steps = **5.8 minutes**, 10.3 per hour. `seg_b_pump_n` (B is a DERELICT-kind section even after the weld): `d_fat` 100, `n_fat` ceil(2001 / 100) = 21 periods = 210 s = **3.5 minutes** (under the 24-period hold, so decay never adds); the HOME figure for comparison is `d_fat` 50, 41 periods without decay, 30 periods = 5.0 minutes with it. G-0 workload *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier 3.8 % mean omitted the return legs and the unplated hull clock)*: per patch, a 180-tick channel plus the walk to the anchor and back at `V_WALK` 2 (hub = the cryo door sill; routes in DA-29; the two cryo-bay leaks share one trip). The home leaks are CLT-LEAK and FUEL-DRIP always, and O2-SHIP only once the player has opened `v_o2_cryo` (it is a player-armed clock). Per 450 s cycle: channels 3 x 3 s = 9 s and walking (672 + 1,248 px) / 2 px per tick = 960 ticks = 16.0 s, **25 s = 5.6 %** (6.8 % if the two cryo leaks are patched on separate trips); with O2-SHIP unopened it is two patches, 22 s = **4.9 %**. The unplated `seg_b_pump_n` adds one patch per 210 s: a 3 s channel and a round trip of about 1,630 px from the home airlock hub (`[derived]` from the weld layout, D.13; Layout's read governs) = 13.6 s of walking, 16.6 s per cycle = **7.9 %**. Totals: **4.9 to 6.8 % with the plate carried, 12.8 to 14.7 % without it**, against the 3.8 % of the earlier figure. **Phase table** (patches falling due in any 2-minute window; the steady-state cycle is phase-locked to the moments the player patched, so the home leaks all share the 7.5-minute cycle and the hull clock a 3.5-minute one): home leaks only, patched within the same 2 minutes: up to 3 due together (2 without O2-SHIP), using 25 to 31 s of the window; plated: 3; unplated: up to 4 (3 home + 1 hull), using 42 to 48 s of 120 s (35 to 40 %); with the stagger lever below: 2. **Stagger lever (Authoring-owned, a content edit):** the only phase Authoring sets is the first deadline of each leak. `P_CRYO_RAD_HP` (FAILED at 8.3 minutes) and `PF_FEED_HP` (FAILED at 10.5) are already 2.2 minutes apart; raising `P_CRYO_RAD_HP` toward 3999 would collide them, lowering it toward 3500 (FAILED at 5.3 minutes) widens the spread to 5.2 minutes; O2-SHIP's deadline is armed by the player, so the lever is the spread of the first two. After the first patches the phase belongs to the player, who can de-synchronise the leaks by patching the cryo pair together and the engine fitting later. The X-DA-9 pass line is recalibrated to this load (Game Feel). `[UNV: Interaction]` one channel is assumed to restore `hp` to `PATCH_CAP_BP`, and `[UNV: Graph]` a PATCH on a fatigued segment at `hp` 6999 (above the cap) gains nothing, so only a FULL repair stops the fatigue (EC below).

**Home while away (the rule and `T_AWAY_SAFE_MIN`)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The home ship is loaded in every legal set (S0 to S4, DA-32), so its clocks run while the player is on a sortie: load-dormancy (DA-13 (a)) never applies to home. `T_AWAY_SAFE_MIN` is the earliest unattended FAILED after every home leak was freshly patched: `T_AWAY_SAFE_MIN = PATCH_DECAY_STEPS + n_dec x WEAR_PERIOD_STEPS + ceil((PATCH_CAP_BP - n_dec x d_decay - HP_FAIL) / (d_decay + D_LEAK_HOME)) x WEAR_PERIOD_STEPS`, because patch decay keeps running after the re-leak (LSG D.9: decay applies while `hp_cap` is below `HP_FULL`) and the re-opened leak adds its gated wear (`D_LEAK_HOME` 16). At the defaults: 2,400 + 21 x 100 + ceil((6000 - 2100 - 3000) / 116) x 100 = 2,400 + 2,100 + 800 = **5,300 steps = 8.8 minutes** (the pipe at `hp` 3,900 re-leaks at 7.5 minutes and is FAILED 8 periods later); for CLT-LEAK the SCRAM follows about 0.6 minutes after FAILED (LSG D.15). **Check against the longest authored dwell:** the reference sortie loop `W_LOOP` 300 s (3,000 steps, Boarding & Salvage) passes with 2,300 steps to spare; a player who patches at departure and then spends A's whole warm window (D.22, 7,768 steps) plus transit does not, and can come home to a FAILED leak and, for CLT-LEAK, a SCRAM. That outcome is accepted as stated, not hidden: it is a recoverable SCRAM (V1 then V12, DA-16), never a death; each patch's PATCH_WEEP has been live at home since the patch was made (LSG R15), so its age is a perceivable fact before the player leaves; and the treadmill cycle is published (7.5 minutes). Whether P2 wants a remote tell as well (for example a home-state read from the pod) is the creative-director's call at X-DA-9 (OQ-DA-51). AC-DA-154 asserts the figure; AC-DA-136 logs every return to a FAILED home leak.

### D.16 Derelict A bus on its battery (the pump-stall window)

The `bus_on_battery_window` formula is defined as:

`C_bus = stock(b_a2) + (stock(b_a1) if j_a_res is closed, else 0)` ; `t_bus_steps = floor(C_bus / R_served)` ; `R_served = sum of requests served on the bus` ; `PUMP_TELL_OK iff t_bus_steps >= PUMP_TELL_MIN_STEPS` ; `PUMP_TELL_FLOOR_STEPS = W_MIN_WARNING_S x TICK_RATE / 6` (= 100 steps = 10 s) ; `PUMP_TELL_FAIRNESS_OK iff t_bus_steps >= PUMP_TELL_FLOOR_STEPS` (the CI lint `PUMP_TELL_UNDER_FAIRNESS`, ERROR, on the conservative `R_served`; the 900-step target is a design intent reported beside it, so P2 is protected by the build and not by the arithmetic of this example)

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `C_bus` | int dJ | 180,000 to 2,490,000 (`b_a2` at its 1,950,000 ceiling plus `b_a1` 540,000) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | the batteries connected to the bus: authored `b_a2` `stock` 1,620,000 dJ (DA-19), plus `b_a1` 540,000 dJ when the tie `j_a_res` is closed | Stock of the batteries that feed A's bus *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `R_served` | int W = dJ per step | 20 to 1600 | LSG D.6 tiers; DA-20 | Scrubber `scr_a` 600, pump `pump_a` 400, `htr_a` 500, five bus lamps 100 (the residual lamp `lamp_a_life` is on its own island while `j_a_res` is open; closing the tie adds its 20 W to the bus) |
| `PUMP_TELL_MIN_STEPS` | int steps | 900 (90 s) | Authoring knob | Service time the pump-stall tell needs to be seen (the design target; reported beside the computed figure, WARN `PUMP_TELL_BELOW_TARGET` when under) |
| `PUMP_TELL_FLOOR_STEPS` | int steps | 100 (10 s) | `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10 s (registry) x `TICK_RATE` 60 / 6 ticks per Graph step | The fairness floor; below it `PUMP_TELL_UNDER_FAIRNESS` is an ERROR (AC-DA-134) |

**Output Range:** 112 steps (the smallest cell, full bus) to 27,000 (the residual lamp alone, D.6).
**Example** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `b_a2` is 1,620,000 dJ, moved off the 1,440,000 floor of its range, and `C_bus` is now summed over the connected batteries)*, with `j_a_main` swapped or patched and closed:

| Configuration | `C_bus` (dJ) | `R_served` (W) | `t_bus_steps` |
|---|---|---|---|
| `j_a_res` OPEN, full bus (scrubber 600, pump 400, heater 500, five lamps 100) | 1,620,000 | 1,600 | **1,012 steps = 101.2 s**: the 900-step target is met with 112 steps to spare |
| `j_a_res` CLOSED, full bus (adds `lamp_a_life` 20 W; `b_a1` joins the pool) | 2,160,000 | 1,620 | 1,333 steps = 133.3 s; the residual lamp's own 45-minute clock is gone, it now drains the shared pool |
| `j_a_res` OPEN, `scr_a` cut (V8) | 1,620,000 | 1,000 | 1,620 steps = 162 s |
| `j_a_res` OPEN, pump alone | 1,620,000 | 400 | 4,050 steps = 6.75 minutes |

`PUMP_TELL_UNDER_FAIRNESS` evaluates every row at the conservative `R_served`. Assumptions: a FAILED `scr_a` still requests its 600 W (the decided default of DA-47, Graph to confirm, OQ-DA-47) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; the battery discharge cap and tier shedding order are `[UNV: Graph]`; A has no reactor, so the batteries only drain: the bus is a clock, not a base. The earlier `b_a1`-only arrangement (540,000 dJ) gives 337 steps = 33.7 s on the full bus, too short for the stall to be read, which is why `b_a2` exists.

### D.17 Reachable-set subject budget

The `subject_budget_per_set` formula is defined as:

`S_int(S) = F(S) + L_seed(S) + C` ; `S_total(S) = S_int(S) + PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE + ACTORS + HORRORS` ; `SUBJECT_BUDGET_SET iff S_int(S) > INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET or S_total(S) > MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` ; `I-7: INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET + PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE <= floor(3 x MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS / 4)` ; `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS_needed = ceil(4 x (INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET + PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE) / 3)`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `F` | int | 0 to 120 | IA D.10 and IA-37; declared interactables (hardpoints, two face subjects per door, valves, hull segments, sockets) | Home 50 (48 + 2 scrubber slots, DA-47), A 28, B 40 |
| `L_seed` | int | 0 to 20 | DA-26 | Seeded loose placements still on the floor: home 2, A 4, B 3 (falls as the player collects) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G2: B carries three loose placements, so home 2, A 4, B 3)* |
| `C` | int | 0 to 18 | IA D.10 | Cap-stub worst case (at most 6 modules x 3 ports), counted once per set |
| `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE`, `ACTORS`, `HORRORS` (Interaction's `P`, `A`, `H`) | int | 24, 4, 2 | IA D.10, I-7; registry `subject_budget` | 30 together; `H` is `HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX` 2 `[PLACEHOLDER: Horror]` Emitter HORROR subjects (one active plus one transient) |
| `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` | int | 120 `[PLACEHOLDER: Interaction]` | registry (Interaction-owned, IA-37) | Content ceiling for the per-registration-window sum `W_int(p) = F + L_seed + C` at every authored body position, not for `S_int` over a loaded set (registry `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET`, `subject_budget`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* |
| `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` | int | 192 `[PLACEHOLDER: Emitter]` (registry range 128 to 384) | registry (Emitter-owned) | Subject cap |

**Output Range:** `S_int` 70 to 145 and `S_total` 100 to 175 in MVP content as loaded-set sums (the design review's 68 to 143 and 98 to 173 plus the two scrubber slots of DA-47) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G2: B carries three loose placements, so home 2, A 4, B 3)*.
**Example:**

| Set | `F` | `L_seed` | `C` | `S_int` | vs 120 | `S_total` | vs 192 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S0 `{home}` | 50 | 2 | 18 | 70 | pass | 100 | pass |
| S1 `{home, A}` | 78 | 6 | 18 | 102 | pass | 132 | pass |
| S2, S3 `{home, B}` / `{home+B}` | 90 | 5 | 18 | 113 | pass (7 slack) | 143 | pass |
| **S4 `{home+B, A}`** | 118 | 9 | 18 | **145** | **over by 25 as a loaded-set sum (not a registered state, see the D37 paragraph below)** | 175 | pass (17 slack) |

I-7 check: 120 + 24 = 144 = floor(3 x 192 / 4) with `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` 192 `[PLACEHOLDER: Emitter]`, exactly at the limit. Raising the budget to 144 alone breaks I-7 (144 + 24 = 168 > 144) and needs `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` of ceil(4 x 168 / 3) = **224** (a two-owner change, Checkpoint rulings Q4 option 2). The Authoring-only diet (option 3) must remove at least 25 subjects from S4 (145 - 120) to bring `S_int` to 120 (the Edge Cases list the named candidates and their readability cost).

**Cumulative table with the instruments and pod consoles that OQ-DA-38 and OQ-DA-39 ask for (design review 2026-10-27).** Instruments are In-World Instruments' indicative placements (home 9, A 4 plus the second battery reading on A that partners `inst_b_a1_stock`, B 5) and the consoles are the four CONSOLE anchors (POD in `airlock`, `a_dock` and `b_dock`; DOCK in `airlock`, OQ-DA-39); each is an Interaction subject *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. Neither list is authored yet; this is the arithmetic of adopting both.

| Set | `S_int` as authored | + instruments | + consoles | `S_int` with both | vs 120 | `S_total` (+ 30) | vs 192 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S0 `{home}` | 68 | 9 | 2 | 79 | pass | 109 | pass |
| S1 `{home, A}` | 100 | 14 | 3 | 117 | pass (3 slack) | 147 | pass |
| S2, S3 `{home, B}` / `{home+B}` | 111 | 14 | 3 | 128 | **over by 8** | 158 | pass |
| **S4 `{home+B, A}`** | 143 | 19 | 4 | **166** | **over by 46** | **196** | **over by 4** |

I-7 for a budget of 166: 166 + 24 = 190 and ceil(4 x 190 / 3) = **254**, which leaves `S_total` 196 with 58 spare. The 25-subject diet (S4 143 to 118 as authored) is no longer enough, because it leaves 141 against 120 with both lists placed, and S2 and S3 would breach 120 where the diet never reached. **Intended route:** ASK-I1 (register by body adjacency or proximity, so a far welded section does not spend the budget) or a joint raise to about 166 with `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` about 254; the diet is the last resort, because it removes restorable modules from a game whose pillar is that wrecks have usable organs (P5). S4 reachability is never gated *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

**User ruling D37 and the registered neighbourhood (2026-10-27).** Interaction registers interactable subjects by body adjacency or proximity and not by "loaded" (ASK-I1 adopted). Define a neighbourhood N as the player's body plus any body adjacent to it; in the MVP a pod-docked derelict is a separate body (BDY-25) and is not adjacent to the home body, and a welded B belongs to the home body `[UNV: Interaction IA-37, the adjacency predicate]`, and the proximity radius is `[PLACEHOLDER: Interaction]`. The gate becomes `SUBJECT_BUDGET_SET iff S_adj(N) = F(N) + L_seed(N) + C > INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET`, with `C` counted once. Worked example with the DA-47 slots: {home} 50 + 2 + 18 = **70**; {A} 28 + 4 + 18 = **50**; {B unwelded} 40 + 3 + 18 = **61**; {home+B} 90 + 5 + 18 = **113**, so the worst case is 113, at most 120 with 7 slack, and S4 `{home+B, A}` (145 as a sum) is never a registered state *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G2: B carries three loose placements, so home 2, A 4, B 3)*. With the instruments and consoles of OQ-DA-38 and OQ-DA-39 the neighbourhoods are {home} 70 + 9 + 2 = 81 (the DOCK console is the second), {A} 50 + 5 + 1 = 56 and {home+B} 113 + 14 + 3 = **130, over by 10** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, so the {home+B} rows wait for Interaction's proximity radius and not for a budget raise. The Emitter side is unchanged: `S_total` stays judged on the loaded set, 145 + 30 = 175 of 192 as authored (17 spare) and 168 + 30 = 198 with both lists (145 + 19 + 4 = 168, 6 over), which is one more reason those rows are not authored yet. The I-7 and cumulative arithmetic above is the record of the pre-ruling route and used 68, 100, 111, 111 and 143 (add 2 to each for DA-47); figures before 2026-10-28: every S2, S3 and S4 sum is one higher (112, 112 and 144; B carried a fourth loose placement) Registry `subject_budget` (Interaction-owned) states the lint as the registration-window form `W_int(p) = F + L_seed + C <= INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` 120 at every authored body position, summed over the registration window plus its one-hop ring (`SUBJECT_WINDOW_HOPS`), with the S4 WARN retired and `P + Y <= PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE` 24 (`Y` the salvage-yield ITEM subjects); where this paragraph says "neighbourhood N" or "adjacency", read that window, and the loaded-set `S_int` formula above is the INFO upper bound only *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.

### D.18 Thermal drift (the cold-room invariant)

The `thermal_drift` formula is defined as (a lint and report formula, harness-side; the natural logarithm is forbidden on the sim path and nothing hashed depends on it):

`tau_steps = floor(100 x V x C_TILE / (K_HULL x S_ext))` = `floor(12000 x V / S_ext)` at the defaults ; `t_cross_min = tau_steps x ln((T0 - T_AMB_MC) / (ONSET_MC - T_AMB_MC)) / 600` for `T0 > ONSET_MC` ; `COLD_ROOM_DRIFT iff t_cross_min < COLD_ROOM_SAFE_MIN` (sealed, unheated, doors closed) ; `COLD_ROOM_UNDECLARED iff T0 <= ONSET_MC and the room is not the declared lethal room`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `V` | int tiles | 4 to 200 | LY D.3 | Room volume |
| `S_ext` | int tiles | 1 to 80 | DA-7 `exposed_tiles` (`THERMAL_INPUT_MISSING` if absent) | Hull tiles exposed |
| `C_TILE`, `K_HULL` | int | 3000 / 25 | LSG D.7 | Thermal mass per tile (dJ per C) and hull conductance |
| `T0` | int mC | authored | C.1 to C.3 | Initial room temperature |
| `T_AMB_MC` | int mC | -30000 | LSG (R14; MVP exterior sink) | Exterior temperature |
| `ONSET_MC` | int mC | -20000 | SE D.4 (`COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC` -15000 less `INSUL_MC` 5000 `[PLACEHOLDER: Suit]`) | Shifted cold onset, at full suit integrity (at or above `SUIT_LEAK_HP`); a leaking suit moves the onset toward `COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC` -15000 (SE-21), and `COLD_ROOM_DRIFT` also prints the -15000 projection |
| `COLD_ROOM_SAFE_MIN` | int min | 25 (range 20 to 60) | Authoring knob (Checkpoint rulings Q7; not registry) | Required loaded minutes above the onset. Closed doors let unheated rooms cool to COOL or FROST within 20 to 30 minutes, and that is the intended consequence of closed-door play, not a warning: default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20) (D12) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |

**Output Range:** 0 to unbounded minutes; the room never falls below `T_AMB_MC` + ceil(100000 / (`K_HULL` x `S_ext`)) (the plate-term stall).
**Example (design values, doors closed):**

| Room | `V` | `S_ext` | `T0` (mC) | `tau_steps` | `t_cross_min` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `a_dock` | 20 | 13 | -6000 | 18,461 | **26.9** |
| `a_spine` | 33 | 11 | -12000 | 36,000 | 35.3 |
| `a_life` | 30 | 11 | -10000 | 32,727 | 37.8 |
| `a_machine` | 35 | 13 | -22000 | 32,307 | below the onset: declared lethal (D.10) |
| `b_dock` | 20 | 13 | 22000 | 18,461 | 50.7 |
| `b_spine` | 39 | 16 | 30000 | 29,250 | 87.3 |
| `b_reactor` | 40 | 21 | 42000 | 22,857 | 75.2 (cold-drift form only; the post-V12 heat projection is the paragraph below) |
| `b_pump` | 20 | 8 | 38000 | 30,000 | 95.8 |
| `b_breach` | 30 | 17 | 10000 | 21,176 | 48.9 (vacuum; reported, not a conduction case) |

`a_dock` is the tightest room at 26.9 minutes against 25 (margin 1.9); at the -15000 projection of a leaking suit it falls to 14.5 minutes (`a_spine` 10.9), printed and reviewed, not a lint threshold. With a door open, `K_OPEN` conduction mixes `a_spine` toward `a_machine`, and the figure falls: the lint prints the open-door projection beside every closed-door row. `a_spine` at -12000 is below `FREEZE_WARN_MC` -10000, so any PIPE there would carry a permanent FREEZE pre-stage warning; A's corridor is authored with no pipes for that reason.

**Warm-up of a heated room (the cryo bay after V3, DA-45; harness-side, derived `[derived]`).** `gross_mC_per_step = floor(1000 x P_dJ / (V x C_TILE))` with `P_dJ` = 500 W x 0.1 s x 10 = 500 dJ per Graph step ; `q_hull_dJ = K_HULL x S_ext x |T - T_AMB_MC| / 100000` ; `net_mC_per_step = floor(1000 x (P_dJ - q_hull_dJ) / (V x C_TILE))` ; `warm_steps = ceil((T_COMFORT_MC - T0) / mean net)`. For `cryo_bay` (`V` 42, `S_ext` 13): C = 42 x 3000 = 126,000 dJ per degree C, gross 500 x 1000 / 126,000 = 3.97 mC per step (about 4); `q_hull` at -2000 mC is 25 x 13 x 28,000 / 100,000 = 91 dJ (net 3.25 mC) and at 10000 mC is 25 x 13 x 40,000 / 100,000 = 130 dJ (net 2.94 mC), mean about 3.1; from -2000 to the COMFORT floor 10000 mC (LSG:96) is 12,000 mC, so about 3,900 Graph steps = 390 s = **about 6.5 minutes**. LSG:839's 2.5 minutes (500 x 1000 / 60,000 = 8.3 mC per step) is the Graph's example for a 20-tile room (60,000 = 20 x `C_TILE`), not this 42-tile bay. Routed to the Graph owner: LSG:839's figure assumes V 20 (no value changes). Authoring keeps the slower climb (Game Feel) rather than retune the verbatim fixture; the only lever, a second heater, is the PWR-OVL trigger (`CRYO_HEATER_COUNT`, Tuning Knobs).

**Heat projection after a reactor restart (`b_reactor`, DA-28; `[derived; Graph owner to confirm Q_src_room]`).** `T_eq_hot = T_AMB_MC + ceil(Q_src_room x 100000 / (K_HULL x S_ext))` ; `t_onset_steps = ceil(tau_steps x ln((T_eq_hot - T0) / (T_eq_hot - HEAT_DMG_ONSET_MC)))` when `T_eq_hot` > `HEAT_DMG_ONSET_MC`, else never ; `e = T_eq_hot - HEAT_DMG_ONSET_MC` feeds D.10 with Suit's heat gain. For `b_reactor` (`S_ext` 21, `tau_steps` 22,857, `Q_src_room` 516 dJ per step, the Graph's D.8 worked example `[UNV: Graph]`): `T_eq_hot` = -30000 + ceil(51,600,000 / 525) = -30000 + 98,286 = **68,286 mC**; `t_onset_steps` = ceil(22,857 x ln(26,286 / 23,286)) = 2,770 steps (**4.6 minutes**); `e` 23,286 is above `TL_DEPTH_CAP_MC` 20,000, so `gain` 40, `runs_critical` ceil(4000 / 40) = 100 (10.0 s) and `runs_failed` 300 (30.0 s). A `T0` of 45000 would put the room on the onset from step 0 and damaging with every uncaptured dJ, which is why the authored value sits 3000 mC under it. **Tell lead (the presentation step before the onset; `[derived]`).** `t_tell_steps = ceil(tau_steps x ln((T_eq_hot - T0) / (T_eq_hot - HEAT_TELL_STEP_MC)))` ; `lead_steps = t_onset_steps - t_tell_steps` ; `HEAT_TELL_LEAD_OK iff lead_steps >= W_MIN_WARNING_S x STEPS_PER_S` with `STEPS_PER_S` = `TICK_RATE` / 6 = 10 (Graph hook divisor 6; D.15's 2400 steps = 4 minutes). For `b_reactor` with `HEAT_TELL_STEP_MC` 44,700: `t_tell_steps` = ceil(22,857 x ln(26,286 / 23,586)) = 2,477 (4.1 minutes after V12), `lead_steps` = 2,770 - 2,477 = **293 (29.3 s, 1,758 ticks)**, above the 100-step floor; the step may be set no higher than 44,897 mC (lead 100) and the authored value leaves a margin of 193 steps. If `T_eq_hot` is at most `HEAT_DMG_ONSET_MC` the room never damages and the lead is not required.

### D.19 Cryo-bay and ship air clocks

The `air_clock` formula is defined as *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the leak is summed per wear period, because `d_cryo_exposed` wears while it leaks)*:

`t_band_steps = ceil((P0_DKPA - p_band) x V x GAS_SCALE / (crew x R_B))` (sealed bay, valve closed, door closed) ; `leak_mgu(hp) = ceil(LEAK_MGU_MAX x (LEAK_HP - hp) / LEAK_HP)` with `hp_k = hp0 - D_LEAK x k` for wear period k = 0, 1, 2 ... counted from the moment the valve opens ; `drain_k = leak_mgu(hp_k) + VENT_HOLD - R_SCR_served` per step, held for `WEAR_PERIOD_STEPS` 100 steps ; `t_pool_steps` = the smallest n with the sum over k < n of 100 x `drain_k` at or above `pool_mGU` (the per-period leak sum) ; `t_destroy_periods = ceil(hp0 / D_LEAK)` (the duct, D.14)

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `P0_DKPA`, `p_band` | int dkPa O2 | 210; THIN 180, HYPOXIC 140, ANOXIC 100 | LSG D.15 O2 bands | Normal and band boundaries |
| `V` | int tiles | 42 (`cryo_bay`) | C.1 | Room volume |
| `GAS_SCALE` | int | 1000 | LSG D.2 | mGU per dkPa per tile |
| `crew`, `R_B` | int | 1 to 2; 300 mGU per step per crew | LSG D.15 fixture | Occupants and respiration |
| `pool_mGU` | int | 3,000,000 (`ot_main`) | DA-35 | Authored O2_TANK stock |
| `LEAK_MGU_MAX`, `LEAK_HP` | int | 500 mGU per step; 4000 bp | LSG D.2; registry | The leak at `hp` 0 and the threshold; `leak_mgu` is 250 at `hp` 2000 and rises toward 500 |
| `D_LEAK` | int bp per period | 16 (HOME) | `LEAK_WEAR_BP_PER_100`, LSG D.9, applied only while the duct leaks (gated) | The duct wears while it leaks, so the leak grows 2 mGU per step each period |
| `VENT_HOLD` | int mGU per step | 300 | LSG D.15 O2-SHIP fixture `[verified by the systems-designer; Graph owner to confirm]` | The vents holding 210 dkPa |
| `R_SCR_served` | int mGU per step | 0 (unserved) or 400 | IDB:212 and LSG D.5 (default 400); the 200 at IDB:190 and IDB:705 is the severed-port leak floor, so ASK-G3 is answered by inspection (the Item DB and Graph owners confirm); 200 is kept as the conservative reading | Scrubber contribution when served |

**Output Range:** the sealed clock exists only while someone breathes in the sealed bay (leaving through the door stops it); the pool clock runs from the moment the valve is opened (V5) and not before, because the isolated duct holds its `hp` (D.14, gated).
**Example:** sealed, one crew: THIN ceil(30 x 42 x 1000 / 300) = 4,200 steps = **7.0 minutes**, HYPOXIC 9,800 steps = 16.3 minutes, ANOXIC 15,400 steps = 25.7 minutes; two crew halve each (co-op hook, Edge Cases). Pool drain after V5, with the leak rising 2 mGU per step each period (period 0: 250 + 300 = 550 unserved): unserved **5,008 steps = 8.3 minutes**; served at `R_SCR` 400 (net 150 + 2k) **11,405 steps = 19.0 minutes**; served at the conservative `R_SCR` 200 (net 350 + 2k) 7,137 steps = **11.9 minutes**. The constant-leak figures of the earlier text (5,455 steps = 9.1 minutes unserved, 8,572 = 14.3 at 200, 20,000 = 33.3 at 400) held the leak at its `hp` 2000 value and are withdrawn; the Graph's own fixture (LSG D.15 O2-SHIP, 5,455) is a constant-leak simplification and the corrected 5,008 sits 8.2 % below it, inside +-15 %. The duct wears only while it leaks, so when the pool empties the leak stops with it: the duct then holds 1,200 `hp` (unserved, 50 periods) or 176 (served, 114 periods) and is not DESTROYED (125 periods = 20.8 minutes, D.14, needs a pool that outlasts it); a zero-part PATCH before the pool empties (`hp` to `PATCH_CAP_BP` 6000, at or above `LEAK_HP`) clears the leak and stops the drain; that patch is the G-0 route with `scr_main` served (with the scrubber unserved, breathing alone empties the pool in 10,000 steps = 16.7 minutes, the Graph's own fixture) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

### D.20 Derelict B's hull-fatigue clock

The `hull_fatigue_clock` formula is defined as:

`periods_to_breach = ceil((hp0 - (BREACH_HP - 1)) / d_fat)` ; `A_j = ceil(A_HOLE_MAX x (BREACH_HP - hp_j) / BREACH_HP)` with `hp_j = BREACH_HP - 1 - d_fat x j` ; `m = smallest j such that 0.01 x sum of A_i for i = 1..j >= ln(P0 / P_band)` ; `periods_to_decomp = periods_to_breach + m`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `hp0` | int bp | 6999 (`seg_b_pump_n`, `fatigued`) | DA-11, DA-15 | Authored segment `hp` |
| `d_fat` | int bp per period | 100 (DERELICT) | D.15 | Fatigue wear |
| `A_HOLE_MAX` | int | 64 (range 32 to 128) | LSG D.2 | Hole area at `hp` 0 |
| `ln(P0 / P_band)` | fixed ratio | 0.916 | LSG D.15 HULL-FATIGUE decompression criterion (quoted, not re-derived) | The pressure fall that counts as DECOMP |

**Output Range:** breach after 30 periods (5.0 minutes of B-loaded time); DECOMP after 40 (6.7 minutes).
**Example:** `periods_to_breach` = ceil(3000 / 100) = 30 (`hp` 3,999 at period 30, `A_hole` 1); the hole then grows 2, 4, 5, 7, 9, 10, 12, 13, 15, 17 (exact D.2 area) and the sum passes 91.6 at `m` = 10 (sum 94; the Graph's D.15 shorthand `ceil(1.6 j)` gives the same `m`, sum 92). DECOMP at period 40 = 4,000 steps = **6.7 minutes**, against LSG D.15's DERELICT row of 6.2 minutes (3,750 steps): +6.7 %, inside +-15 %. The clock runs from the first loaded step, is cumulative across visits (dormancy freezes it, LS-17), and never stops after the weld because B stays DERELICT-kind inside the home body; the A hull plate (a FULL repair) stops it for good.

### D.21 Derelict B's restart reservoir floor

The `restart_reservoir_floor` formula is defined as:

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the leak is summed per wear period, the D.19 pattern, because `pb_leak` wears while coolant flows; the constant-leak `n_race`, `W_MAX` and `S0_min` are withdrawn)*

`leak_ml(hp) = min(stock, ceil(LEAK_ML_MAX x (LEAK_HP - hp) / LEAK_HP))` ; `hp_k = hp_restart - D_LEAK_B x k` for flowing period k = 0, 1, 2 ... counted from V12 (step s of the race lies in period k = floor((s - 1) / 100)) ; `drain(n)` = the sum over steps s = 1..n of `leak_ml(hp_k(s))` (the per-period leak sum) ; `S0_min(W) = S_FLOOR + drain(10 x W)`, clamped to the reservoir cap `RES_B_CAP_ML` 20,000 ; `RES_FLOOR iff res_b_stock < S0_min(RES_B_REACTION_WINDOW_S)` ; `W_MAX` = the largest `W` with `drain(10 x W) <= res_b_stock - S_FLOOR` (`RES_FLOOR` also fires when `W` exceeds it) ; `t_floor_steps` = the smallest n with `drain(n) > res_b_stock - S_FLOOR` (the floor-crossing step: the latest a patch may land) ; `t_empty_steps` = the smallest n with `drain(n) >= res_b_stock` (the tank-empty step)

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `LEAK_ML_MAX` | int mL per step | 20 | LSG D.2 | Leak at `hp` 0 |
| `hp_restart` | int bp | 2900 at build | DA-17 (`pb_leak`) | The dead leak's `hp` when the pump first flows (V12) |
| `S_FLOOR` (this GDD's name) | int mL | 7,500 `[UNV: Graph D.8 circ-vs-level curve]` | read from LSG D.15's CLT-LEAK row (12,500 of 20,000 mL lost leaves `circ` about 437 permille) | Stock below which the loop heats toward OVERHEAT |
| `W` | int s | 90 (45 to 120) | Authoring knob `RES_B_REACTION_WINDOW_S`, legal only up to `W_MAX` | Seconds to find the frost fan and complete a patch (3 s channel) after V12; `W_MAX` is 123 at the authored 16,000 mL by the per-period sum (the constant-leak 141 is withdrawn) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `res_b_stock` | int mL | 16,000 (authored) | DA-35 | RESERVOIR `stock_ml` |
| `D_LEAK_B` | int bp per period | 32 (DERELICT) | `LEAK_WEAR_BP_PER_100` x the DERELICT factor (DA-13 (b)), gated: only while coolant flows (D.14) | `pb_leak`'s wear after V12, so the leak grows 1 mL per step about every 6 periods *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |

**Output Range:** `S0_min` 0 to 20,000 mL; the authored stock must cover the floor plus the window's summed leak. At the authored values `t_floor_steps` is 1,238 (123.8 s, about 2.1 minutes after V12) and `t_empty_steps` 2,112 (about 3.5 minutes); `W_MAX` is 123.
**Example** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: recomputed with the per-period sum; the constant-leak figures of the design-review text, `n_race` 21 periods = 3.5 minutes, `S0_min(90)` 12,900 mL, margin 3,100 and `W_MAX` 141, ignored the coolant already drained and are withdrawn)*: `pb_leak` at `hp` 2900: `leak_ml` = ceil(20 x 1100 / 4000) = ceil(5.5) = **6 mL per step** at V12, rising as the leak wears 32 bp per flowing period: 6 mL per step in periods 0 to 3, 7 in 4 to 9, 8 in 10 to 15, 9 in 16 to 21 (100 steps per period). Summed: 2,400 mL after period 3, 6,600 after 9, 8,200 after 11, 9,000 after 12. The margin over the floor is 16,000 - 7,500 = 8,500 mL, so **the floor is crossed at step 1,238** (8,200 + 8 x 38 = 8,504; 123.8 s, about 2.1 minutes after V12) and the tank is empty at step 2,112 (period 21, `pb_leak` then at about `hp` 2,228). `S0_min(90)` = 7,500 + `drain(900)` 5,900 = **13,400 mL** (margin 2,600 under the authored 16,000). The same sum explains why 2200 was rejected: `leak_ml` 9 at build and no slack. Under the gated reading (binding since 2026-10-27, D.14) the dry leak holds `hp` 2900 until V12, so V12 first is feasible at any time; **the race is the window after V12: the patch must land inside the `W` window (90 s, 33.8 s of margin) and in every case before step 1,238**; patching first and then V12 remains the safest order (`SCRAM_RESTART_STEADY` uses it). **If the patch never lands,** the stock passes the floor at step 1,238 and the loop heats toward OVERHEAT; with the Graph's heat-to-SCRAM lag of about 350 steps (the LSG D.15 CLT-LEAK row: `T_mach` above 70 C, OVERHEAT 50 steps later, SCRAM 300 after that) `r_b` re-SCRAMs at about step 1,588 (2.6 minutes) after V12 `[UNV: Graph D.8 circ-vs-level curve]`; the harness asserts it after step 1,238 and by step 1,826 (+15 %). A patch landing after the floor (for example at step 1,300, stock 7,000 mL) does not prevent the re-SCRAM, because nothing refills the stock short of a top-up (V7). **Window range:** `S0_min(W)` is 10,250 mL at 45 s, 13,400 at 90, 15,700 at 120 and 15,940 at 123; 124 s needs 16,020 and 140 s needs 17,300, so the 16,000 mL stock is legal for `W` up to 123 and the knob range is 45 to 120. **The cap cannot buy a long race:** at `RES_B_CAP_ML` 20,000 the floor is crossed at step 1,723 (2.9 minutes), so a 3.5-minute race is out of reach by stock at this leak growth. **The race is set by the X-DA-10 pass bar, not by a derived constant** (creative-director, siding with the game-designer): the shipping default is the honest 2.1 minutes, and if X-DA-10 fails because the race is too short the levers, in order, are the stock (to the cap, 2.9 minutes) and then the leak growth, which Authoring holds only through `pb_leak`'s authored `hp`. Lever table (`t_floor_steps` at 16,000 / 20,000 mL; the class limit is `HP_FAIL` 3000, because above it `pb_leak` stops being a dead fault, DA-17):

| `pb_leak` `hp` at V12 | `leak_ml` at V12 | `t_floor_steps`, 16,000 mL | `t_floor_steps`, 20,000 mL | `t_empty_steps`, 16,000 mL |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **2900 (authored)** | 6 | **1,238 (2.1 min)** | 1,723 (2.9 min) | 2,112 (3.5 min) |
| 3000 (the dead-fault class limit) | 5 | 1,326 (2.2 min) | 1,826 (3.0 min) | 2,223 (3.7 min) |
| 3300 (a live DEGRADED leak, out of class) | 4 | 1,643 (2.7 min) | 2,213 (3.7 min) | 2,650 (4.4 min) |
| 3500 (out of class) | 3 | 1,917 (3.2 min) | 2,529 (4.2 min) | 3,013 (5.0 min) |

So a race of 3.5 minutes or more needs both the cap and a change of `pb_leak`'s fault class (a live leak with a live trail on B) or of the Graph's leak growth; that is a design change routed to the X-DA-10 result (OQ-DA-50), not taken here (R10). **`pb_leak`'s 15.2 minutes to DESTROYED are flowing minutes:** B's own reservoir supplies at most 21 flowing periods (empty at step 2,112, `hp` about 2,228), and on B alone the pump stops earlier with the re-SCRAM, so DESTROYED is reached only if the flow is kept up by top-ups (V7); `DEAD_FAULT_STILL_REPAIRABLE` drives it with a refilled reservoir *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

**B timeline (the first visit on one page)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. B's first visit stacks a new verb (V12), the restart race, the heat tell, the breach and decompression on two clocks: B-loaded time (from the first loaded step, D.20) and time after V12 (D.21, D.18). The example column assumes V12 at 600 steps (60 s) after arrival, an illustrative pace, not a target.

| Event | Clock | Step on its clock | B-loaded step if V12 is at 600 |
|---|---|---|---|
| arrival at `arrive_b_dock`; hull fatigue starts (D.20) | B-loaded | 0 | 0 |
| V12 (the hand crank, 4 s channel); `pb_leak` starts to flow and wear | after V12 | 0 | 600 |
| the `W` window ends (comfort target for the patch) | after V12 | 900 | 1,500 |
| `res_b` crosses `S_FLOOR` (the latest the patch saves the restart) | after V12 | 1,238 | 1,838 |
| re-SCRAM if never patched (`[UNV: Graph D.8]`) | after V12 | about 1,588 | about 2,188 |
| `seg_b_pump_n` breach (hull fatigue) | B-loaded | 3,000 | 3,000 |
| heat tell at `b_reactor` (`HEAT_TELL_STEP_MC`, D.18) | after V12 | 2,477 | 3,077 |
| heat damage onset at `b_reactor` | after V12 | 2,770 | 3,370 |
| DECOMP in `b_pump` | B-loaded | 4,000 | 4,000 |

The safe order (patch `pb_leak`, then V12) is the counter-intuitive one, so AC-DA-137 logs which order each tester chose; an optional crank precursor on the home reactor (a V12 taught at home before B) is a possible scaffold, not authored here (OQ-DA-50).

### D.22 Heater on the bus battery (A's warm window) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: new formula)*

The `bus_heater_window` formula is defined as (a lint and report formula, harness-side; the natural logarithm is forbidden on the sim path and the lint uses the fixed-point table of D.18):

`dT_step = 1000 x (P_htr - q_hull(T)) / (V x C_TILE)` mC per step in fixed-point accumulation (each step's quotient carries its remainder into the next and only the accumulated `T` is floored for comparison; a per-step floor would give 4 mC instead of 4.51 at the start and shorten the full-bus window to about 10.9 minutes, which the table below does not use) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* with `q_hull(T) = K_HULL x S_ext x (T - T_AMB_MC) / 100000` ; `t_onset_steps` = the first step at which `T` exceeds `ONSET_MC` while the heater runs ; `T_end` = `T` after `t_bus_steps` (D.16) ; `t_return_steps = ceil(tau_steps x ln((T_end - T_AMB_MC) / (ONSET_MC - T_AMB_MC)))` (the room cools back with the heater dead) ; `window_steps = max(0, t_bus_steps - t_onset_steps) + t_return_steps` ; `THAW_REACHABLE iff T_end > FREEZE_OFF_MC`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `P_htr` | int W = dJ per step | 500 | `htr_a`, `mod_heater` (all of it is heat, `waste_permille` 1000; other loads' waste is ignored, the conservative side) | Heat into `a_machine` |
| `V`, `S_ext`, `C_TILE`, `K_HULL` | int | 35, 13, 3000, 25 | C.2, D.18 | Room volume, exposed tiles, thermal mass, hull conductance |
| `T0`, `ONSET_MC`, `FREEZE_OFF_MC` | int mC | -22000, -20000, -10000 | C.2; SE D.4; LSG | Start, shifted cold onset, thaw line |
| `tau_steps` | int steps | 32,307 | D.18 | Cooling time constant of `a_machine` |
| `t_bus_steps` | int steps | per D.16 | D.16 | How long the battery feeds the heater |

**Output Range:** `t_onset_steps` is 447 (the heater lifts the room the 2,000 mC to the onset in 44.7 s); `window_steps` runs from 7,768 (12.9 minutes) on the full bus to 28,144 (46.9 minutes) with the heater alone.
**Example** (`b_a2` 1,620,000 dJ, 1000 mC = 1 degC):

| Bus configuration | `t_bus_steps` | `T_end` (mC) | Gain | `t_return_steps` | Window out of damaging cold |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| full bus, `j_a_res` open | 1,012 | -17,502 | 4.5 C | 7,203 (12.0 min) | 7,768 steps = **12.9 min** |
| `scr_a` cut | 1,620 | -14,867 | 7.1 C | 13,385 | 14,558 = 24.3 min |
| pump and heater only | 1,800 | -14,096 | 7.9 C | 14,990 | 16,343 = 27.2 min |
| heater alone (scrubber, pump and lamps shed) | 3,240 | -8,083 | 13.9 C | 25,351 | 28,144 = 46.9 min; `THAW_REACHABLE` |
| full bus, `j_a_res` CLOSED (`b_a1` joins) | 1,333 | -16,105 | 5.9 C | 10,628 | 11,514 = 19.2 min |

Reading: waking the branch buys about 12.9 minutes of unhurried work in the one lethal room, and the pump lantern going dark at step 1,012 is the cue that it ends (the room is back at the onset 12.0 minutes after the stall). It buys nothing else: the scrubber is cut either way, the splice needs no power, and the benefit is bounded by the bus battery and not by the ship. Stated plainly *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: every in-room act already fits the cold-room work budget without heat (DA-28, D.10), and the reference sortie cuts `htr_a`, which ends the window, so the window enables no act that is otherwise impossible; it buys margin (a fumbled swap, a second look, a slower cut) and nothing more, which is the deliberately modest payoff of user ruling D45, measured by AC-DA-135 with curiosity-only wakes excluded. The window opens only once the room passes the onset, 447 steps (44.7 s) after the breaker closes, and `j_a_main` is inside the room, so the swap is made, the breaker closed and the room left for about 45 s (DA-28). The earlier "about +4 degC in the 90 s window" understated it by leaving out that the room starts only 2 degC under the onset. **Thaw:** `pa_frozen` thaws (above `FREEZE_OFF_MC`) only on the heater-alone row, which needs the scrubber and the pump cut and every lamp switched off (three kinds of act), so it is not an authored route; it is accepted and tagged: with the pump shed there is no flow, so under gated wear the thawed pipe holds `hp` 2500, and the thaw-burst roll of LS-11 is the Graph's `[UNV: Graph]`. With the pump served the thaw needs 2,496,600 dJ, which is 6,600 dJ above the largest pool the knobs allow (`b_a2` at its 1,950,000 ceiling plus `b_a1` 540,000 with `j_a_res` closed = 2,490,000), so a pump-served thaw (and the 13.2-minute wear clock behind it) is out of reach by construction; lint `THAW_REACHABLE` (ERROR, CI) evaluates it on the pooled `C_bus`, `j_a_res` closed, and fails the build if a knob edit makes it reachable *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. 'Too high' for `A_BUS_BATTERY_DJ` is therefore not 'A becomes a base' (a full 3,600,000 dJ cell lasts 2,250 steps = 3.75 minutes on the full bus) but that ceiling. The decision whether this computed window is a sufficient reason to wake the branch, or whether an authored payoff is wanted as well, is OQ-DA-43.

### D.23 Authored discretionary dwell on the reference route (Boarding D.1, D73) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: new formula, routed by Boarding & Salvage, G2/R21)*

The `dwell_budget` formula is defined as (a lint and report formula, harness-side; no sim path reads it):

`dwell_s = n_witness x W_READ_S + n_decide x DECIDE_S + n_door x DOOR_PAUSE_S + n_light x LIGHT_PAUSE_S` ; `DWELL_BUDGET_LOW iff dwell_s < A_DWELL_BUDGET_MIN_S`, where the four counts are taken over the reference route to `htr_a` and back (a_dock arrival, `a_spine`, `door_a_spine_machine`, `a_machine`, and home by the same doors), each authored source counted once

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `n_witness` | int | 0 to 40 | the DA-34 consequence-channel witnesses (a trail, a frost line, a lamp on or off, undisturbed dust, a gauge) in the rooms of the route, plus the visible unknown if it lies on the route | A player reads each authored witness once |
| `n_decide` | int | 0 to 12 | authored decision points: a junction with two or more legal routes of different cost (the `a_spine` junction, DA-48), the breaker-or-leave choice at `j_a_main`, the cut-or-leave-dark choice, the tell decision at the decision reach (DA-48), the drop-and-outwait choice | Counted once each |
| `n_door` | int | 0 to 8 | doors on the route whose state is the player's choice (open on the way in, carry through, shut behind) | Counted once each |
| `n_light` | int | 0 to 12 | authored dark rooms or stretches on the route that need a lamp, stick or beam decision (A is dark by construction) | Counted once each |
| `W_READ_S`, `DECIDE_S`, `DOOR_PAUSE_S`, `LIGHT_PAUSE_S` | int s | 8, 15, 5, 8 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Authoring knobs (Tuning Knobs) | Seconds of discretionary dwell per source of each kind; playtest-calibrated at the first X-DA run |
| `A_DWELL_BUDGET_MIN_S` | int s | 190 (150 to 240) `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Authoring knob; derived from Boarding D.1: 300 s of W_LOOP less the 108.2 s W_LOOP with the survey allowance at 28 tiles = 191.8 s, rounded down; 190 s is the authored minimum the lint guarantees; Boarding D.1 / OQ-BS-28 (d) states the lint-guaranteed W_ABOARD as about 229 s and the design expectation as about 259 s *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | The least authored dwell the route must produce |

**Output Range:** `dwell_s` 0 to about 600 s; the lint prints the estimate and every source (kind, room, seconds). Pass at 190 s or more.
**Example:** the level-designer's current content plan for the reference route is read as 6 witnesses (48 s) + 2 decision points (30 s) + 2 doors (10 s) + 2 light pauses (16 s) = **104 s**, inside the 90 to 110 s the level-designer estimated; the lint's own recount of the shipped templates governs. **It fails the lint**: 104 is 86 s under 190, so the content plan must add dwell sources (about 86 s more, for example more witnesses and decision points along the lengthened `a_spine` of DA-48); this is a content-plan gap and not a rule change (OQ-DA-56). A plan summing to 189 s fires the lint and 190 s does not (for example 189 = 1 x 15 + 6 x 5 + 18 x 8, and 190 = 2 x 15 + 20 x 8).

## Edge Cases

Every case states the exact outcome and, where an owner decides, names the owner; "lint" always means the CONTENT lint of DA-40 unless a Layout id is cited. Cases that depend on an open ask say which branch binds until it is answered. Figures are the Formulas section's.

**Zero, maximum and out-of-range authored values**

- **If a room authors zero fixtures**: `FIXTURE_COUNT_ROOM` ERROR naming the room, its `V` and the bound (`fixtures_min` 1); a dark housing counts, so the fix is adding a housing, not a powered lamp. A room with no lamp housing has no way to be re-lit or un-lit, which breaks restraint (P3) (D.3, EM-13).
- **If a room authors more fixtures than `min(6, 1 + floor_div(V, 8))`** (for example a third lamp in a `V` 20 dock): `FIXTURE_COUNT_ROOM` ERROR with the count and bound; no auto-trim. The bound is Layout's D.16 `[PLACEHOLDER: Emitter AC-58]`; a retune by the Emitter reruns the lint against the new text through `TUNING_SOURCE_DRIFT`.
- **If `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` is retuned inside its registered range 7500 to 36000**: at 14,400 or below every dead fault still passes; above it `DEAD_FAULT_NOT_DRIED` fails `pa_frozen`, `j_a_main`, `pb_leak` and `seg_b_breach`, naming Failure & Trail as owner (the intended tripwire, D.11). Back-dating to -(36000 + 2400) = -38400 covers the whole range, but 38,400 steps (1 h 4 min) of `since_steps` at step 0 is outside what F&T registers as a trail input, so F&T must confirm it clamps rather than extrapolates `[UNV: F&T]`; recommended only with the Graph's confirmation (the sign is accepted, LSG:182, OQ-DA-1).
- **If `fail_since_step` is negative** (the shipping case: M1 `instantiate_section` accepts a non-positive value, LSG:182, amended 2026-10-27): dead faults are back-dated at build and are dried on arrival. **Contingency if that line reverts and M1 rejects it:** dead faults cannot be back-dated; fallbacks in order: a Graph `dead_since` template field; an in-template back-dated trail age that F&T reads in place of `since_steps` (OQ-DA-1); or authoring the fault live and letting `since_steps` accrue (the first 20 loaded minutes), which breaks "dried on arrival" and keeps FT AC-44 BLOCKED. The BOOT subset's `DEAD_FAULT_NOT_DRIED` stays valid on either field *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If any authored number is a JSON float** (for example `2161.0` or `0.15`): `AUTHORED_NON_INTEGER` ERROR in CI; at load the cast-and-bound-check refuses a non-integral value and never rounds silently, because the engine's JSON parser returns floats `[UNV: 4.7.2, spike]`. Percent never appears: charges are dJ (DA-4).
- **If an authored `hp` is outside 0 to `HP_FULL` 10000 or a `stock` is negative**: the loader's bound check refuses the template whole at BOOTING (Layout's whole-template rejection, LY-30); the offline lint reports the range before the build can ship.
- **If `cab_trunk` `cap` is authored outside 2159 to 2163 W**: `OVERLOAD_WINDOW` ERROR naming the failing beacon state, its `rho_pm` and `dhp_ovl` (2150 W fails with the beacon on: rho 1116, 5 bp per step; 2000 W gives rho 1190 and DESTROYED in 71 s). The window is solved by D.9, so the fix is the number, never a lint waiver.
- **If a segment is authored at `hp` 0 (`seg_b_breach`) and Failure & Trail answers that a DESTROYED subject emits no descriptor**: the open hole cannot be drawn from the Graph's hull row (a P1 lie by omission) and the dried-trail count thins by one. Fallback order (Checkpoint rulings Q9): `seg_b_breach` at `hp` 1500 (FAILED, BREACH mode, a descriptor exists, patchable with zero parts, B's traverse kept because the room stays authored at vacuum); then a dead non-leaky power fault on a branch V12 never serves. Never a fault that wakes on V12, which would be a ninth live clock and break `SCRAM_RESTART_STEADY` (OQ-DA-11).
- **If home LOADs reach 12** (one more lamp, motor or heater): `NODE_COUNT_DRIFT` WARN, because 11 is already over the target 8 by 3 and the tolerance is `max(floor(8 x 20 / 100), 3)` = 3 (D.2). One more LOAD also adds 20 W to the trunk (the standing load 1880 W changes and D.9 must be re-solved) and a hardpoint.

**Faults, clocks and staging**

- **If the player never opens `v_o2_cryo`** (the shipping closed-valve staging, gated LSG D.9): `d_cryo_exposed` holds `hp` 2000 and nothing happens to it at any idle time (LSG:127, LSG:515); `G0_ZERO_PART_SURVIVABLE` passes the case, and the only cost of never opening the valve is the cryo bay's own air for a player who stays there door-shut (THIN at 7.0 minutes, next bullet). If the cited Graph text reverts, the valve-OPEN contingency of DA-16 applies *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If the player closes a valve upstream of any LEAK-mode subject** (V5 used as a lever): `intensity_pm` drops to 0 (the drip, frost growth and hiss stop), mode LEAK stays, and `hp` is held (gated LSG D.9, DA-16); re-opened, the leak resumes from the stored `hp` (LSG:762). The authored levers (DA-15 "State at build and lever") are written for that reading *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If the player stays in the sealed `cryo_bay`** (door CLOSED, `v_o2_cryo` CLOSED, one crew): the bay's O2 reaches THIN at 7.0 minutes, HYPOXIC at 16.3 and ANOXIC at 25.7; leaving through the door ends the clock because the corridor has served vents. This is a consequence of the closed-valve staging, not a ninth fault, and its tell is the Graph's atmosphere band descriptors; it is the reason the cryo door is a short walk from the bed (D.19).
- **If a heater is installed in `hp_cargo_heater` while `j_cryo` is still open**: trunk load is 1240 + 500 = 1740 W, rho 805, no damage. The overload exists only for the pair (heater installed, cryo branch closed): closing `j_cryo` adds the 640 W branch and lands on 2380 W, rho 1101, the authored PWR-OVL (D.9). The trigger is a player act twice over, so the fault is LATENT in the sense of DA-15 (`FAULT_LEVER_UNREACHABLE` requires both acts reachable in sortie order).
- **If Attention switches the beacon on or off during the PWR-OVL burn**: `bcn_main` toggles 20 W, `rho_pm` moves between 1101 and 1110, `dhp_ovl` stays 4, so the 4.2-minute DESTROYED clock does not change; `set_beacon` off issues M14 on `bcn_main` only and no lamp is zeroed (DA-24).
- **If the cryo branch is re-energised from a full blackout** (every LOAD re-energised at once): requested power is clamped to `S` 2500 W `[PLACEHOLDER: Item DB out_w_rated]`, `cab_trunk` sees rho 1156 for `INRUSH_STEPS` 5 at 9 bp per step: 45 bp of scorch per recovery, so 67 recoveries are needed to cross below `HP_OK`. Breaker cycling is a small cost by design (D.9).
- **If the player closes the seam breaker (the seam CABLE sub-edge's breaker, LS-15; `j_b_main` stays CLOSED) while `r_b` is SCRAMmed** (S3 or S4) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: B's bus (1240 W) hangs below `cab_trunk`, L 3120 W, supply-clamped to S 2500 W, rho 1156, 9 bp per step. The tell is live and immediate: OVERLOAD sev 1 from the first step (the hum climbs), every home lamp dims through the served ratio and the junction box scorches; `cab_trunk` is DEGRADED at step 334, FAILED at 778 (a home blackout) and DESTROYED (`NOT_REPAIRABLE`) at 1,112. **Recovery:** re-open the breaker (V3) before step 778 and a zero-part PATCH restores the trunk; re-open between 778 and 1,111 and patch it once the load is off (a blackout meanwhile); from 1,112 only the last autosave remains, which is the one residual run-ender (DA-20; the interlock fallback is OQ-DA-41). Not a ninth fault, because it is an exogenous player act with a visible tell and a stated recovery; the seam breaker is authored OPEN at weld and the design rule is to close it after V12 (DA-20). `OVERLOAD_WINDOW` prints the figures as a WARN, and `G0_SEAM_CLOSED_ON_DEAD_B` and AC-DA-132 assert them *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If the player switches lamps off while PWR-OVL burns**: the lamps-off table of D.9 applies (k 1 to 5 slow the burn to 3,333 to 10,000 steps to DESTROYED; k 6 and 7 stop the damage while the OVERLOAD hum continues); the fault stays ACTIVE until the heater is removed or the cryo branch is opened, and the authored band is the all-lamps-on build state (AC-DA-141) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If the player runs A's heater alone** (scrubber and pump cut, every lamp switched off): the bus window is 3,240 steps and `a_machine` reaches -8,083 mC, above `FREEZE_OFF_MC`: `pa_frozen` thaws with no flow behind it (the pump is shed), holds `hp` 2500 under gated wear, and the thaw-burst roll of LS-11 is the Graph's `[UNV: Graph]`; with the pump served the thaw is out of reach (D.22). Accepted and tagged, not an authored route *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If a second `mod_scrubber` is ever hung below `cab_trunk`** (user ruling D72, 2026-10-27: the two vacant scrubber slots of DA-47 now accept one, so the earlier statement that scrubber hardpoints are one per section no longer holds at `home_ship`): installing a second RUNNING scrubber with the cryo branch closed adds +600 W and gives rho 1147, 8 bp per step and DESTROYED in 1,250 steps (2.1 minutes). D.9 is re-solved in DA-47: the heater window is unchanged because a vacant slot adds 0 W, and the install states are tabulated there; the lint flags the new window failure, not the player's act *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.
- **If a PATCH is applied to `seg_b_pump_n` at `hp` 6999** (above `PATCH_CAP_BP`): M7 clamps by `hp_cap`, the patch adds nothing and `fatigued` stays set `[UNV: Graph whether hp_cap is lowered]`. Only a FULL repair (the A hull plate) stops the fatigue (LSG:237); Authoring never relies on a pre-breach patch (D.15).
- **If the player welds B without carrying the A hull plate**: `seg_b_pump_n` runs a 3.5-minute treadmill for the rest of the session (about 17 patches per hour at 210 s per cycle); G-0 holds and `WELD_WITHOUT_PLATE_SURVIVABLE` passes at that cadence. The dependency is stated, never gated (anti-pillar: no scripted sortie order).
- **If the player skips the `pb_leak` patch, restarts `r_b` and tries V12 again right after it re-SCRAMs** (Interaction's dead-reactor beat, IA:54): the first V12 succeeds (`T_mach` is below `OVERHEAT_OFF_MC` at build, DA-35); the re-SCRAM follows the floor crossing at step 1,238 (about 1,588 steps, 2.6 minutes, after V12, and by step 1,826, D.21 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*) with `T_mach` above `OVERHEAT_OFF_MC`, and an immediate second V12 fills the bar and returns `BLOCKED(STILL_HOT)` until the machine cools. This is the only STILL_HOT case on B and it is the player's doing, not an eighth or ninth fault.
- **If the player leaves `pb_leak` unpatched past step 1,238 after V12 (123.8 s, about 2.1 minutes)** (the flowing-time window; before V12 the dry leak holds): the summed leak (6 to 8 mL per step, 8,504 mL by then) has taken `res_b` below `S_FLOOR` 7,500 mL, `circ` collapses and `r_b` re-SCRAMs at about step 1,588 (by step 1,826), and a late patch no longer saves it without a top-up (V7). Past 15.2 minutes of flowing time `pb_leak` is DESTROYED and `NOT_REPAIRABLE`, so the restart chain is unreachable until a part is spent; that needs the flow kept up by top-ups, because B's own stock empties at step 2,112 and the re-SCRAM stops the pump earlier (D.21, gated) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.
- **If a dead leaky fault reaches DESTROYED while its section is loaded and its fluid is flowing**: it yields its `scrap_count` of parts on salvage (IDB-29), its edge `cap` is 0 and the dried trail's source is gone; `TRAIL_NO_SOURCE` treats a DESTROYED source as valid (the cause remains). `PART_SUPPLY_EXCEEDS` bounds placements statically, and the headless run counts instances, which may exceed 5 (DA-25).
- **If `pa_frozen` thaws** (the room warms above `FREEZE_OFF_MC` -10000 after the bus wakes `htr_a`): it leaves forced FREEZE and, in mode LEAK at `hp` 2500, wears at 32 bp per period: DESTROYED in 79 periods = 13.2 minutes of flowing time (with the pump shed it holds), and wakes with the coolant. Feeding A is therefore a clock decision twice over (DA-13 (b)). `[UNV: Graph]` whether a forced-FREEZE pipe counts as mode LEAK.
- **If the stored `res_a` or `rad_a` is not forced FREEZE** in `a_machine` (`T0` -22000, below `FREEZE_ON_MC` -20000): it would freeze-fail at load, an unlisted endogenous transition. `FROZEN_ROOM_UNFORCED` ERROR; every liquid-bearing node in any room whose `T0` is below `FREEZE_ON_MC` carries `forced_mode` FREEZE (DA-19, DA-36).
- **If a derelict is loaded for more than `T_SORTIE_LOADED_MAX` minutes idle**: the dry dead leaks hold (`pb_leak`, `pa_frozen`) and only the flow-independent clocks run (`seg_b_pump_n` breached at 5.0 and decompressed at 6.7 minutes of B-loaded time); the headless tests assert that ledger, not a promise that a long stay is safe (D.14, D.20) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

**Simultaneous rules and cross-system collisions**

- **If the player sorties to A after the weld** (S4 `{home+B, A}`): `S_int` is 145 as a loaded-set sum (143 before DA-47's two slots) against `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` 120 `[PLACEHOLDER: Interaction]`, an overshoot of 25; `SUBJECT_BUDGET_SET` reports it as INFO (registered neighbourhoods are the gate, D37) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The Emitter's own cap is not reached (`S_total` 175 of `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` 192 `[PLACEHOLDER: Emitter]`, 17 to spare while actors and horrors stay at 6), so nothing fails dark at runtime today. Unless ASK-I1 (register by proximity or body adjacency) or the joint raise lands, S4 stays a WARN and the diet below is the last resort (D.17, OQ-DA-20) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. **Superseded by user ruling D37 (2026-10-27):** with subjects registered by adjacency, S4's loaded-set sum is 145 after DA-47 (25 over as a sum) but no registered neighbourhood exceeds 113, so the WARN is informational and the budget gate passes with 7 slack (D.17) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G2: B carries three loose placements, so home 2, A 4, B 3)*.
- **If Authoring must cut 24 subjects from S4 without changing another owner's number** (the last resort, ranked after ASK-I1 and the joint raise: it cuts restorable modules against P5 and no longer reaches 120 once the instruments and consoles of D.17 are placed) (Checkpoint rulings Q4 option 3; the Graph confirmed on 2026-10-27 that a VENT or LOAD may be authored bare, LSG:182, so OQ-DA-23 is answered): the named diet is 25 subjects, in this order: (1) **11 vents become bare VENT nodes** with no `def_id` and no hardpoint, like the bare junctions (home 5, A 2, B 4); (2) **11 non-residual wreck lamps become bare housings** on their branch with no swappable module (A 5, B 6); (3) **3 door motors become bare LOAD nodes** (`dm_cryo_s`, `dm_air_hatch`, `dm_b_hatch`). S4 `S_int` falls from 144 to 119, one under the budget (figures before 2026-10-28; 145 to 120 after DA-47 and B's removed placement, exactly at the budget with no slack *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G2: B carries three loose placements, so home 2, A 4, B 3)*). Readability cost: those 25 can no longer be cut, swapped or salvaged, so the module faucet loses 25 low-value instances (no parts, no scrubber, no breaker) and a dead wreck lamp cannot be replaced; switching still works because `set_switch` acts on the emitter. Diet (1) also takes the home-ship from 27 hardpoints to 22, which sits under `MAX_HARDPOINTS_PER_SECTION` 32 with 10 spare; the cap no longer forces the diet (C-4 was closed by the raise to 32), so diet (1) is justified by the S4 subject budget alone (OQ-DA-23). `[UNV: Layout, Graph]` whether a VENT, LOAD or housing node may be authored bare; this is the fallback only. User ruling D37 makes the fallback unnecessary: with DA-47's two slots S4's sum is 145, so the 25-subject diet would leave 120, exactly at the budget with no slack: it clears the loaded-set sum on paper but no longer clears once the instruments and consoles of D.17 are placed (141 against 120); it is withdrawn as a route and kept for the record *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G2: B carries three loose placements, so home 2, A 4, B 3)*.
- **If Interaction accepts ASK-I1**: `SUBJECT_BUDGET_SET` reads the registered set (subjects within body adjacency or proximity) instead of the loaded set, the diet is not applied, and a far welded section no longer spends the budget.
- **If a joint ask is taken instead** (budget 144 with `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` 224 `[PLACEHOLDER: Emitter]`): `S_total` 175 passes with 49 spare (a budget of 145 would be needed after DA-47 and B's removed placement *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G2: B carries three loose placements, so home 2, A 4, B 3)*); the change is two owners', registered by both (D.17). Raising the Interaction budget alone breaks I-7.
- **If the player opens the door between `a_spine` (-12000 mC) and `a_machine` (-22000 mC)**: conduction (`K_OPEN`) equalises toward the midpoint over minutes, `a_machine` warms and the spine cools; "exactly one lethal-cold room" is a **build-state** invariant, so `COLD_ROOM_DRIFT` prints the open-door projection and `a_dock`'s 26.9-minute figure is re-read with that door open (D.18).
- **If a Suit cell is topped up from `b_a1` or `b_b1`**: `withdraw_store` removes the credited stock one for one; the residual lamp's remaining loaded time is `stock / 20` steps and at 0 it dims for one step and goes dark. The honest reading is the player's own spend (P1); Authoring never relies on the lamp as a long-lived beacon (D.6).
- **If the residual lamp's tie junction is closed on a powered bus**: on B (`j_b_res`, a reactor bus) the island joins the bus, the lamp is served by the bus and, once the bus has surplus, the battery recharges at `min(S - sum_served, CH_CAP)` (17.6 minutes from 12 %). On A (`j_a_res`) there is no source (`NO_SOURCE`, D.6): the tie merges `b_a1` into the bus pool, `C_bus` becomes 2,160,000 dJ and `R_served` gains the lamp's 20 W (1,333 steps on the full bus instead of 1,012, D.16), and the residual lamp's own 45-minute clock ends because the lamp now drains the shared pool. The authored tie is OPEN at build so this is a V3 decision, never a free lamp (DA-27) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If a fixture housing, prop or loose slot sits on a fault's maximum floor run**: `LOOSE_ON_TRAIL_RUN` WARN (a slot within 1 tile of a floor run) or VA-5 review failure (a prop or housing); the trail must be readable from across the room (VA-4).

**Degenerate and exploit strategies**

- **If the player powers nothing on A and only cuts `scr_a` by beam**: legal and intended (DA-28's quiet alternative): no lantern lights, no clock starts on the dead branch, the spare breaker stays on its shelf, and the player takes the cut by beam and decides whether the failed scrubber is worth carrying home (under user ruling D72 `scr_a` now has a home destination, the vacant scrubber slots of DA-47, so the carry is a decision and no longer a garbage beat; Boarding & Salvage amends BS-17 and C-BS-6 to match) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Restraint is a real strategy, not a loss state (P3).
- **If the player strips every module node on both wrecks**: nothing respawns (one placement is one take, IDB-29); a wreck stripped bare is a dark place with less in it, not a "cleared" state (no win state). In the MVP, with two wrecks and no procedural generation, this is the end of authored content, not a win; scarcity then pushes inward only through the Vertical Slice's derelicts (OQ-DA-24).
- **If the player farms glowsticks**: 12 are placed once (home 8 in two stacks of 4, A 2, B 2), none respawn, and a carried stack is at most `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX` 4 `[PLACEHOLDER: Item DB]`. The lights-off strategy is not starved because the beam is hands-free (user decision).
- **If the player seals every door and stands in the scrubber room** (the Graph's D1 bot): the room enters THIN as the Graph's criterion asserts; authoring gives no safe farm: scrubbers wear under duty and a derelict scrubber is at 2000 or 6000 `hp` (LSG D1).
- **If the player patches `j_a_main` instead of swapping the spare breaker**: the junction reaches `PATCH_CAP_BP` 6000 and the bus wakes at DEGRADED, WORN trace on; the spare then sits on the shelf for the Vertical Slice. Both routes are legal (G-0, IDB AC-42); the swap is cheaper to keep.
- **If the player carries a cut module (for example `scr_a`) through a room with a live endogenous fault**: speed is `V_CARRY` 1, escape slack at 480 px falls to 1.0 s (60 ticks, with `door_close_ticks` 60), and a route over about 540 px cannot be carried inside the window; `ESCAPE_ROUTE_CARRY` lists the room, as an ERROR when the slack is below 0 (D.5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The stowed hull plate is not a carry (it does not lock `V_CARRY`), but it is not free either: it shows the HEAVY back-frame and, by user ruling D18, disables run while stowed, so the carrier moves at `V_WALK` 2 and the escape-route check for a plate-bearing player uses the walk slack (D.5, AC-DA-149) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.
- **If the player powers A's bus and the battery runs out**: `b_a2` lasts 101 s with the full bus and 162 s with the scrubber cut; then the lanterns go dark, the pump stalls again and `htr_a` stops heating the lethal room, which is back in damaging cold about 12.0 minutes later (D.22). "Feeding the wreck is how you survive staying" has a battery's lifetime, not a base's; A has no reactor (D.16).
- **If the player takes the residual-lamp island battery to power something else**: `b_a1` and `b_b1` are `mod_battery_cell` modules on hardpoints and can be cut like any module; the wreck's unknown goes dark and the exterior view reads it dark (P1: the light had a source and the source left).

**Save, restore, load-dormancy and template edits**

- **If a derelict is unloaded mid-clock** (the pod leaves): its slots are skipped, `hp`, stocks, `warn_since_step` and the lamp's battery freeze exactly, and on return the section resumes with no fast-forward (LS-17). Every loaded-time clock (D.6, D.14, D.20) accumulates across visits, which is why a long absence never costs the player a wreck.
- **If a template is edited after a save exists**: its `template_hash` changes (LY-26). Before the first external build, saves are disposable (SL-14) and the old save is discarded; after it, migration belongs to the owner. A `room_key` rename is committed only with a note in the file header (DA-44).
- **If a negative `fail_since_step` is persisted and restored**: it is a signed persisted node field (LS-3, LS-21) and returns exactly; `since_steps` is recomputed from `step_no`, so a save made after the dried threshold keeps its dried trail and a back-dated fault never "re-wets".
- **If a world is restored from an autosave taken after the weld**: the sockets read WELDED, `mvp_world.json` is consulted only at world build, and no Authoring lint runs at restore; the BOOT subset runs at every boot, and the content of a restored node is the saved value, never the template's initial value (DA-35 is the build diff only).
- **If the player dies and restarts from the last autosave**: every Authoring-owned fact comes back as saved (clocks, stocks, charges); Authoring adds no death rule and no one-way point (DA-29). MVP death handling is Save/Load's and is cited, not restated.
- **If a residual lamp's section has never been built**: the exterior view reads `charge_dJ` > 0 as lit; the first load starts the clock and the persisted stock then governs (D.6, `[provisional: Web Map & Transit undesigned]`).

**Geometry, weld and topology**
